69. In the ninth, ‘the assembly of nobles’ means the gathering or coming together of assemblies of nobles. This method applies everywhere. ‘Many hundreds of noble assemblies’ refers to gatherings like those of Bimbisāra, gatherings of relatives, gatherings of the Licchavis, and others, which are also found in other world-systems. ‘Having conversed before’ means having previously engaged in conversation. ‘Discussion’ means having also previously engaged in discussions on the Dhamma. ‘Such is their appearance’ refers to them being white-skinned, dark-skinned, or tawny-skinned, while the Teacher is golden in color. This is said with reference to their form. And this form is merely what is apparent to them. However, the Blessed One is not like a barbarian, nor does he wear jeweled earrings, but sits in the manner of a Buddha. They see only forms similar to their own. ‘Such is their voice’ refers to them having broken voices, cracked voices, or croaking voices, while the Teacher has a voice like Brahmā’s. This is said with reference to a different language. For even if the Teacher speaks while seated on a royal throne, it occurs to them, “Today the king speaks sweetly.” But when the Blessed One has finished speaking and departs, and they see the king again, they wonder, “Who is this now?” Here, “Who is this now?” means, “Who is this who just now spoke in the Magadhan language or the Sinhalese language with a sweet tone in this place and then disappeared—is it a god or a human?” Even as they wonder thus, they do not know. Why then does he teach the Dhamma to those who do not know? For the sake of habituation. For the Dhamma, even when heard thus, becomes a condition in the future—thus he teaches with reference to the future. The occurrence of ‘many hundreds of brahmin assemblies’ and so forth should be understood in terms of gatherings like that of Soṇadaṇḍa, and so forth, as well as in other world-systems.

10. Bhūmicālasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Earthquake Discourse

70. Dasame nisīdananti idha cammakhaṇḍaṃ adhippetaṃ. Udenaṃ cetiyanti udenayakkhassa vasanaṭṭhāne katavihāro vuccati. Gotamakādīsupi eseva nayo. Bhāvitāti vaḍḍhitā. Bahulīkatāti [V 3.0246] punappunaṃ katā. Yānīkatāti [P 4.0149] yuttayānaṃ viya katā. Vatthukatāti patiṭṭhānaṭṭhena vatthu viya katā. Anuṭṭhitāti adhiṭṭhitā. Paricitāti samantato citā suvaḍḍhitā. Susamāraddhāti suṭṭhu samāraddhā.

70. In the tenth discourse, by 'a seat' is here meant a piece of leather. By 'Udena's shrine' is meant a monastery built at the dwelling place of the yakkha Udena. This same method applies to Gotamaka and others as well. 'Developed' means increased. 'Made much of' means done repeatedly. 'Made a vehicle' means made like a harnessed vehicle. 'Made a foundation' means made like a basis in the sense of a support. 'Established' means determined. 'Accumulated' means thoroughly built up, well-developed. 'Well undertaken' means well begun.

Iti aniyamena kathetvā puna niyametvā dassento tathāgatassa khotiādimāha. Ettha kappanti āyukappaṃ. Tasmiṃ tasmiṃ kāle yaṃ manussānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ, taṃ paripuṇṇaṃ karonto tiṭṭheyya. Kappāvasesaṃ vāti “appaṃ vā bhiyyo”ti vuttavassasatato atirekaṃ vā. Mahāsīvatthero panāha – “buddhānaṃ aṭṭhāne gajjitaṃ nāma natthi, punappunaṃ samāpajjitvā maraṇantikavedanaṃ vikkhambhento bhaddakappameva tiṭṭheyya. Kasmā pana na ṭhitoti? Upādinnakasarīraṃ nāma khaṇḍiccādīhi abhibhuyyati, buddhā ca khaṇḍiccādibhāvaṃ appatvā pañcame āyukoṭṭhāse bahujanassa piyamanāpakāleyeva parinibbāyanti. Buddhānubuddhesu ca mahāsāvakesu parinibbutesu ekakeneva khāṇukena viya ṭhātabbaṃ hoti daharasāmaṇeraparivārena vā, tato ‘aho buddhānaṃ parisā’ti hīḷetabbataṃ āpajjeyya. Tasmā na ṭhito”ti. Evaṃ vuttepi yo pana vuccati “āyukappo”ti, idameva aṭṭhakathāya niyāmitaṃ.

Having spoken thus without restriction, he then demonstrates with restriction, saying, 'The Tathāgata...' and so on. Here, 'kappa' means a lifespan-kappa. At whatever time, whatever the lifespan of humans may be, he could remain, making it complete. 'Or the remainder of a kappa' means more than the hundred years mentioned in the phrase 'a little or more.' However, the Elder Mahāsīva says: 'There is no such thing as an unfounded boast for the Buddhas. Having repeatedly entered attainment and suppressed the death-bound sensation, he could indeed remain for the entire auspicious kappa. But why did he not remain? The appropriated body is overcome by such things as having broken teeth. And the Buddhas, not having reached the state of having broken teeth and so on, pass into final Nibbāna in the fifth portion of their lifespan, precisely when they are dear and pleasing to the multitude. Moreover, when the great disciples who have understood following the Buddha have passed into final Nibbāna, he would have to remain alone like a tree stump, or with an entourage of young monks and novices. Then, it would lead to a state of being censured, with people saying, 'Oh, what a following the Buddha has!' Therefore, he did not remain.' Even though this was said, what is called 'a lifespan-kappa' is precisely what is determined in the commentary.

Yathā taṃ mārena pariyuṭṭhitacittoti ettha tanti nipātamattaṃ, yathā mārena pariyuṭṭhitacitto ajjhotthaṭacitto aññopi koci puthujjano paṭivijjhituṃ na sakkuṇeyya, evameva [P 4.0150] nāsakkhi paṭivijjhitunti attho. Māro hi [M 3.0248] yassa sabbena sabbaṃ dvādasa vipallāsā appahīnā, tassa cittaṃ pariyuṭṭhāti. Therassa ca cattāro vipallāsā appahīnā, tenassa māro cittaṃ pariyuṭṭhāsi. So pana cittapariyuṭṭhānaṃ karonto kiṃ karotīti? Bheravaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ vā dasseti, saddārammaṇaṃ vā sāveti. Tato sattā taṃ disvā vā sutvā vā satiṃ vissajjetvā vivaṭamukhā honti, tesaṃ mukhena hatthaṃ pavesetvā hadayaṃ maddati, tato visaññāva hutvā tiṭṭhanti. Therassa panesa mukhe hatthaṃ pavesetuṃ kiṃ sakkhissati, bheravārammaṇaṃ pana dassesi. Taṃ disvā thero nimittobhāsaṃ nappaṭivijjhi. Bhagavā jānantoyeva kimatthaṃ yāva tatiyaṃ āmantesīti? Parato “tiṭṭhatu, bhante bhagavā”ti yācite “tuyhevetaṃ dukkaṭaṃ, tuyhevetaṃ aparaddha”nti dosāropanena so katanukaraṇatthaṃ.

In 'as one whose mind is obsessed by Māra,' the word 'taṃ' is merely a particle. The meaning is that just as some other worldling whose mind is obsessed and overwhelmed by Māra would be unable to penetrate, so too was he unable to penetrate. For Māra obsesses the mind of one for whom the twelve perversions are, in every way, unabandoned. In the Elder's case, four perversions were unabandoned; therefore Māra obsessed his mind. But when he obsesses the mind, what does he do? He shows a terrifying visual object or makes one hear a terrifying sound object. Then, beings, having seen or heard it, abandon mindfulness and become open-mouthed. Thrusting his hand through their mouths, he crushes their heart; then they stand there senseless. But as for the Elder, how could this one thrust a hand into his mouth? He did, however, show a terrifying object. Seeing it, the Elder did not penetrate the overt hint. Though the Blessed One knew, why did he address him up to a third time? It was for the purpose of lessening his grief later, when he would be requested, 'May the Blessed One remain, venerable sir!', by placing the blame on him, saying, 'This is your wrongdoing, this is your offense.'

Māro pāpimāti ettha satte anatthe niyojento māretīti māro. Pāpimāti tasseva vevacanaṃ[V 3.0247]. So hi pāpadhammasamannāgatattā “pāpimā”ti vuccati. Kaṇho, antako, namuci, pamattabandhūtipi tasseva nāmāni. Bhāsitā kho panesāti ayañhi bhagavato sambodhipattiyā aṭṭhame sattāhe bodhimaṇḍeyeva āgantvā “bhagavā yadatthaṃ tumhehi pāramiyo pūritā, so vo attho anuppatto, paṭividdhaṃ sabbaññutaññāṇaṃ, kiṃ te lokavicāraṇenā”ti vatvā yathā ajja, evameva “parinibbātu dāni, bhante bhagavā”ti yāci. Bhagavā cassa “na tāvāha”ntiādīni vatvā paṭikkhipi. Taṃ sandhāya – “bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante”tiādimāha.

In 'Māra the Evil One,' Māra is so called because he engages beings in what is harmful and destroys their virtue. 'Pāpimā' is a synonym for him. For he is called 'Pāpimā' because he is endowed with evil qualities. 'And this was spoken'—for on the eighth week after the Blessed One's attainment of full enlightenment, Māra approached him right at the Bodhi-maṇḍala and said: 'Blessed One, the purpose for which you fulfilled the perfections has been achieved. You have penetrated omniscient knowledge—what need is there for you to concern yourself with the world?' Having said this, just as today, he then entreated, 'Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One pass into final Nibbāna!' The Blessed One refused him, saying, 'Not yet,' and so on. Referring to this, it is said: 'And this was spoken, venerable sir,' and so on.

Tattha [P 4.0151] viyattāti maggavasena byattā, tatheva vinītā, tathā visāradā. Bahussutāti tepiṭakavasena bahu sutaṃ etesanti bahussutā. Tameva dhammaṃ dhārentīti dhammadharā. Atha vā pariyattibahussutā ceva paṭivedhabahussutā ca. Pariyattipaṭivedhadhammānaṃyeva dhāraṇato dhammadharāti evamettha attho daṭṭhabbo. Dhammānudhammappaṭipannāti ariyadhammassa anudhammabhūtaṃ vipassanādhammaṃ paṭipannā. Sāmīcippaṭipannāti anucchavikapaṭipadaṃ paṭipannā. Anudhammacārinoti anudhammaṃ caraṇasīlā. Sakaṃ ācariyakanti attano ācariyavādaṃ. Ācikkhissantītiādīni sabbāni aññamaññavevacanāni. Sahadhammenāti sahetukena sakāraṇena vacanena. Sappāṭihāriyanti yāva niyyānikaṃ katvā dhammaṃ desessanti.

Here, 'viyattā' means skilled by way of the path, likewise trained, and likewise confident. 'Bahussutā' means they are learned because they have heard much by way of the three Piṭakas. They uphold that very Dhamma, hence they are 'upholders of the Dhamma' (dhammadharā). Alternatively, they are learned in both textual learning and penetration. Because of their retention of the Dhammas of both textual learning and penetration, they are called 'upholders of the Dhamma'—thus, the meaning here should be understood in this way. 'Dhammānudhammappaṭipannā' means they practice the insight Dhamma, which is in accordance with the noble Dhamma. 'Sāmīcippaṭipannā' means they practice the fitting path. 'Anudhammacārino' means they are of the nature to conduct themselves in accordance with the Dhamma. 'Sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ' refers to their own teacher's doctrine. 'Ācikkhissanti' and so on are all synonyms for each other. 'Sahadhammena' means with a reasoned and causal statement. 'Sappāṭihāriyaṃ' means they will teach the Dhamma having made it leading to liberation.

Brahmacariyanti [M 3.0249] sikkhāttayasaṅgahitaṃ sakalaṃ sāsanabrahmacariyaṃ. Iddhanti samiddhaṃ jhānassādavasena. Phītanti vuddhipattaṃ sabbapāliphullaṃ viya abhiññāsampattivasena. Vitthārikanti vitthataṃ tasmiṃ tasmiṃ disābhāge patiṭṭhitavasena. Bāhujaññanti [P 4.0152] bahūhi ñātaṃ paṭividdhaṃ mahājanābhisamayavasena. Puthubhūtanti sabbākārena puthulabhāvappattaṃ. Kathaṃ? Yāva devamanussehi suppakāsitanti, yattakā viññujātikā devā ceva manussā ca atthi, sabbehi suṭṭhu pakāsitanti attho. Appossukkoti nirālayo. Tvañhi pāpima aṭṭhamasattāhato paṭṭhāya “parinibbātu dāni, bhante bhagavā, parinibbātu sugato”ti viravanto āhiṇḍittha. Ajja dāni paṭṭhāya vigatussāho hohi, mā mayhaṃ parinibbānatthaṃ vāyāmaṃ karohīti vadati.

'The holy life' means the entire holy life of the Dispensation, comprised of the threefold training. 'Successful' means accomplished by way of delighting in the bliss of jhāna. 'Thriving' means having attained growth by way of the attainment of supernormal knowledge, like a tree fully blossomed. 'Widespread' means spread out by way of being established in every direction. 'Known to many' means known and penetrated by many, by way of the realization of the great populace. 'Expansive' means having attained a state of broadness in every way. How so? 'Well proclaimed by gods and humans'—this means that as many intelligent beings as there are among gods and humans, all have well proclaimed it. 'Be without anxiety' means without striving. For you, O Evil One, from the eighth week onwards, have been wandering about, crying out, 'Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Nibbāna! Let the Sugata attain final Nibbāna!' From today onwards, be without zeal. Do not make an effort for my final Nibbāna—thus he speaks.

Sato sampajāno āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossajjīti satiṃ sūpaṭṭhitaṃ katvā ñāṇena paricchinditvā āyusaṅkhāraṃ vissajji pajahi. Tattha na bhagavā hatthena leḍḍuṃ viya āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossaji, temāsamattameva [V 3.0248] pana phalasamāpattiṃ samāpajjitvā tato paraṃ na samāpajjissāmīti cittaṃ uppādesi. Taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ “ossajī”ti. Ussajītipi pāṭho. Mahābhūmicāloti mahanto pathavīkampo. Tadā kira dasasahassī lokadhātu kampittha. Bhiṃsanakoti bhayajanako. Devadundubhiyo ca phaliṃsūti devabheriyo phaliṃsu, devo sukkhagajjitaṃ gajji, akālavijjulatā nicchariṃsu, khaṇikavassaṃ vassīti vuttaṃ hoti.

'Mindful and clearly comprehending, he relinquished the life-formation' means that having well-established mindfulness and having ascertained with wisdom, he released and abandoned the life-formation. There, the Blessed One did not relinquish the life-formation as if throwing away a clod of earth with his hand. Rather, having entered the fruition attainment for only three months, he then generated the thought, 'I will not enter it again after this.' Referring to this, it is said, 'he relinquished.' 'Ussaji' is also a reading. 'The great earthquake' means a great trembling of the earth. Indeed, at that time, the ten-thousandfold world-system shook. 'Frightful' means fear-generating. 'And the divine drums resounded' means the divine drums sounded, the sky uttered a dry thunder, untimely lightning flashed forth, and a momentary rain fell—so it is meant.

Udānaṃ udānesīti kasmā udānesi? Koci nāma vadeyya “bhagavā pacchato pacchato anubandhitvā ‘parinibbātu, bhante’ti upadduto bhayena āyusaṅkhāraṃ vissajjesī”ti, tassokāso [P 4.0153] mā hotu, bhītassa hi udānaṃ nāma natthīti pītivegavissaṭṭhaṃ udānaṃ udānesi.

'He uttered an utterance'—why did he utter it? Lest someone should say, 'The Blessed One, being followed closely behind and harassed with "Venerable sir, attain final Nibbāna!", relinquished the life-formation out of fear,' let there be no opportunity for that. For one who is afraid, there is no such thing as an utterance. Thus, he uttered the utterance, released by the force of joy.

Tattha sabbesaṃ soṇasiṅgālādīnampi paccakkhabhāvato tulitaṃ paricchinnanti tulaṃ. Kiṃ taṃ? Kāmāvacarakammaṃ. Na tulaṃ, na vā tulaṃ sadisamassa aññaṃ lokiyaṃ kammaṃ atthīti atulaṃ. Kiṃ taṃ? Mahaggatakammaṃ. Atha vā kāmāvacaraṃ rūpāvacaraṃ tulaṃ, arūpāvacaraṃ atulaṃ. Appavipākaṃ vā tulaṃ, bahuvipākaṃ atulaṃ. Sambhavanti sambhavahetubhūtaṃ, rāsikārakaṃ piṇḍakārakanti [M 3.0250] attho. Bhavasaṅkhāranti punabbhavasaṅkhāraṇakaṃ. Avassajīti vissajjesi. Munīti buddhamuni. Ajjhattaratoti niyakajjhattarato. Samāhitoti upacārappanāsamādhivasena samāhito. Abhindi kavacamivāti kavacaṃ viya abhindi. Attasambhavanti attani sañjātaṃ kilesaṃ. Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti – savipākaṭṭhena sambhavaṃ, bhavābhisaṅkharaṇaṭṭhena bhavasaṅkhāranti ca laddhanāmaṃ tulātulasaṅkhātaṃ lokiyakammañca ossaji, saṅgāmasīse [P 4.0154] mahāyodho kavacaṃ viya attasambhavaṃ kilesañca ajjhattarato hutvā samāhito hutvā abhindīti.

Therein, 'measurable' (tulaṃ) means that which is weighed and determined by being directly experienced by all, even dogs and jackals. What is that? It is kamma of the sensuous sphere. 'Immeasurable' (atulaṃ) means it is not measurable, or there is no other worldly kamma similar to it. What is that? It is exalted kamma. Alternatively, sensuous-sphere and form-sphere kamma are 'measurable', while formless-sphere kamma is 'immeasurable'. Or, kamma with little fruit is 'measurable', while kamma with much fruit is 'immeasurable'. 'Arising' (sambhavaṃ) means being the cause of arising, meaning it is an accumulator, an aggregator. 'Formations of becoming' (bhavasaṅkhāraṃ) means that which fabricates renewed existence. 'He relinquished' (avassaji) means he abandoned. 'The sage' (muni) means the Buddha-sage. 'Delighting inwardly' (ajjhattarato) means delighting in one's own interior. 'Composed' (samāhito) means composed by way of access and absorption concentration. 'He shattered like armor' (abhindi kavacamiva) means he shattered it as if it were armor. 'Self-arisen' (attasambhavaṃ) means defilement arisen in oneself. This is what is meant: He relinquished worldly kamma, known as 'measurable and immeasurable', which is named 'arising' (sambhavaṃ) because it has a result and 'formations of becoming' (bhavasaṅkhāraṃ) because it fabricates becoming. And like a great warrior at the head of battle shatters his armor, he, having become one who delights inwardly and is composed, shattered the self-arisen defilement.

Atha vā tulanti tulento tīrento. Atulañca sambhavanti nibbānañceva sambhavañca. Bhavasaṅkhāranti bhavagāmikammaṃ. Avassaji munīti “pañcakkhandhā aniccā, pañcannaṃ khandhānaṃ nirodho nibbānaṃ nicca”ntiādinā (paṭi. ma. 3.37-38) nayena tulayanto buddhamuni bhave ādīnavaṃ, nibbāne ca ānisaṃsaṃ disvā taṃ khandhānaṃ mūlabhūtaṃ bhavasaṅkhāraṃ kammaṃ “kammakkhayāya saṃvattatī”ti (ma. ni. 2.81; a. ni. 4.232-233) evaṃ vuttena kammakkhayakarena ariyamaggena avassaji. Kathaṃ? Ajjhattarato samāhito, abhindi kavacamivattasambhavaṃ. So hi vipassanāvasena ajjhattarato, samathavasena samāhitoti evaṃ pubbabhāgato paṭṭhāya [V 3.0249] samathavipassanābalena kavacamiva attabhāvaṃ pariyonandhitvā ṭhitaṃ, attani sambhavattā “attasambhava”nti laddhanāmaṃ sabbakilesajālaṃ abhindi. Kilesābhāvena ca kataṃ kammaṃ appaṭisandhikattā avassaṭṭhaṃ nāma hotīti evaṃ kilesappahānena kammaṃ pajahi. Pahīnakilesassa ca bhayaṃ nāma natthi, tasmā abhītova āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossajji, abhītabhāvañāpanatthañca udānaṃ udānesīti veditabbo.

Alternatively, 'measurable' (tulaṃ) means weighing and investigating. 'Immeasurable and arising' (atulañca sambhavañca) means Nibbāna and becoming, respectively. 'Formations of becoming' (bhavasaṅkhāraṃ) means kamma leading to another existence. 'The sage relinquished': The Buddha-sage, weighing by the method beginning with 'the five aggregates are impermanent; the cessation of the five aggregates, Nibbāna, is permanent', and seeing the danger in becoming and the benefit in Nibbāna, relinquished that kamma which is the formations of becoming and the root of the aggregates, by the Noble Path that, as stated, 'leads to the destruction of kamma'. How? Delighting inwardly, composed, he shattered the self-arisen like armor. For he delighted inwardly by way of insight, and was composed by way of serenity. Thus, from the preliminary stage onwards, by the power of serenity and insight, he shattered the entire net of defilements, which is named 'self-arisen' because it arises in oneself, and which stands enveloping the individual existence like armor. And due to the absence of defilements, kamma, because it does not produce a new life-linking, is called 'relinquished'. Thus, by the abandonment of defilements, he abandoned kamma. And for one whose defilements are abandoned, there is no such thing as fear. Therefore, being fearless, he relinquished the life-formation. And to make known his fearlessness, he uttered an utterance. Thus it should be understood.

Yaṃ mahāvātāti yena samayena yasmiṃ vā samaye mahāvātā. Vāyantīti upakkhepakavātā nāma [P 4.0155] uṭṭhahanti, te vāyantā saṭṭhisahassādhikanavayojanasatasahassabahalaṃ udakasandhārakavātaṃ upacchindanti, tato ākāse udakaṃ bhassati, tasmiṃ bhassante pathavī bhassati, puna vāto attano balena antodhammakaraṇe viya udakaṃ ābandhitvā gaṇhāti, tato udakaṃ uggacchati, tasmiṃ uggacchante pathavī uggacchati. Evaṃ udakaṃ kampitaṃ [M 3.0251] pathaviṃ kampeti. Etañca kampanaṃ yāvajjakālāpi hotiyeva, bahubhāvena pana ogacchanuggacchanaṃ na paññāyati.

'When the great winds' (yaṃ mahāvātā) means at whatever time the great winds. 'They blow' (vāyanti) means winds called 'tossing-up' arise. These winds, blowing, cut through the water-supporting wind that is 960,000 yojanas thick. Then water falls in the space below, and when it falls, the earth falls. Again, the wind, by its own power, as if inside a bellows, binds and seizes the water. Then the water rises, and when it rises, the earth rises. Thus, the agitated water makes the earth tremble. And this trembling continues even to the present day, but the falling and rising are not perceived due to their great extent.

Mahiddhikā mahānubhāvāti ijjhanassa mahantatāya mahiddhikā, anubhavitabbassa mahantatāya mahānubhāvā. Parittāti dubbalā. Appamāṇāti balavā. So imaṃ pathaviṃ kampetīti so iddhiṃ nibbattetvā saṃvejento mahāmoggallāno viya, vīmaṃsanto vā mahānāgattherassa bhāgineyyo saṅgharakkhitasāmaṇero viya pathaviṃ kampeti. Saṅkampetīti samantato kampeti. Sampakampetīti tasseva vevacanaṃ. Iti imesu aṭṭhasu pathavikampesu paṭhamo dhātukopena, dutiyo iddhānubhāvena, tatiyacatutthā puññatejena, pañcamo ñāṇatejena, chaṭṭho sādhukāradānavasena, sattamo kāruññasabhāvena, aṭṭhamo ārodanena. Mātukucchiṃ okkamante ca tato nikkhamante ca mahāsatte tassa puññatejena pathavī akampittha, abhisambodhiyaṃ ñāṇatejābhihatā hutvā akampittha, dhammacakkappavattane sādhukārabhāvasaṇṭhitā sādhukāraṃ dadamānā akampittha, āyusaṅkhāraossajjane [P 4.0156] kāruññasabhāvasaṇṭhitā cittasaṅkhobhaṃ asahamānā akampittha, parinibbāne ārodanavegatunnā hutvā akampittha. Ayaṃ panattho pathavidevatāya vasena veditabbo. Mahābhūtapathaviyā panetaṃ natthi acetanattā. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānatthamevāti.

'Great in power, great in might' means: great in power due to the greatness of success, and great in might due to the greatness of the dominion to be experienced. 'Limited' means weak. 'Boundless' means strong. 'He makes this earth tremble' means that, having produced psychic power, he makes the earth tremble, like the Great Moggallāna when inspiring awe, or like the novice Saṅgharakkhita, the nephew of the Elder Mahānāga, when investigating his own power. 'Shakes' means causes to tremble all around. 'Causes to quake' is a synonym for that. Thus, among these eight earth tremors, the first is due to the disturbance of the elements, the second due to the power of psychic potency, the third and fourth due to the power of merit, the fifth due to the power of knowledge, the sixth by way of giving approval, the seventh due to a state of compassion, and the eighth due to lamentation. When the Great Being descended into his mother's womb and when he emerged from it, the earth trembled due to the power of his merit. At his full enlightenment, it trembled, being struck by the power of his knowledge. At the turning of the Wheel of Dhamma, it trembled, being established in a state of giving approval and giving approval. At the relinquishing of the life-formation, it trembled, being established in a state of compassion and unable to bear the agitation of mind. At the final passing away, it trembled, being struck by the force of lamentation. This meaning, however, should be understood by way of the earth deity. For the great elemental earth, this is not so, due to its insentience. The rest is clear in meaning everywhere.

Bhūmicālavaggo sattamo.

The Seventh Chapter on Earthquakes.

(8) 3. Yamakavaggo

(8) 3. The Chapter on Pairs

1-2. Saddhāsuttadvayavaṇṇanā

1-2. The Commentary on the Two Discourses on Faith

71-72. Aṭṭhamassa [V 3.0250] paṭhame no ca sīlavāti na sīlesu paripūrakārī. Samantapāsādikoti samantato pasādajanako. Sabbākāraparipūroti sabbehi samaṇākārehi samaṇadhammakoṭṭhāsehi paripūro. Dutiye santāti paccanīkasantatāya santā. Vimokkhāti paccanīkadhammehi vimuttattā ca vimokkhā.

In the first of the eighth, 'but not virtuous' means not a fulfiller in virtues. 'Completely inspiring' means generating inspiration in every way. 'Perfect in all aspects' means complete with all the aspects of a recluse and with the divisions of a recluse's qualities. In the second, 'peaceful' means peaceful due to the stilling of opposing defilements. 'Liberations' means liberations due to being freed from opposing things.

3-9. Maraṇassatisuttadvayādivaṇṇanā

3-9. Commentary on the Discourses Beginning with the Two on Mindfulness of Death

73-79. Tatiye [M 3.0252] bhāvetha noti bhāvetha nu. Sāsananti anusiṭṭhi. Āsavānaṃ khayāyāti arahattaphalatthāya. Catutthe patihitāyāti paṭipannāya. So mamassa antarāyoti so mama jīvitantarāyopi, puthujjanakālakiriyaṃ karontassa saggantarāyopi maggantarāyopi assa. Satthakā vā me vātāti satthaṃ viya aṅgamaṅgāni kantantīti satthakā. Pañcamādīni vuttanayāneva. Navame saṃsaggārāmatāti pañcavidhe saṃsagge ārāmatā.

In the third, `bhāvetha no` means `bhāvetha nu` (should you develop?). 'Teaching' means instruction. 'For the destruction of the taints' means for the sake of the fruit of arahantship. In the fourth, 'applied' means entered upon. 'That would be an obstacle for me' means: that would be an obstacle to my life, and for one dying as an ordinary person, an obstacle to heaven and an obstacle to the path. 'Knife-like winds' means winds that cut the limbs like a weapon. The fifth and following are in the same way as has been explained. In the ninth, 'delighting in company' means delighting in the five kinds of association.

10. Kusītārambhavatthusuttavaṇṇanā

10. The Commentary on the Discourse on the Grounds for Laziness and Exertion

80. Dasame [P 4.0157] kusītavatthūnīti kusītassa alasassa vatthūni patiṭṭhā, kosajjakāraṇānīti attho. Kammaṃ kattabbaṃ hotīti cīvaravicāraṇādikammaṃ kattabbaṃ hoti. Na vīriyaṃ ārabhatīti duvidhampi vīriyaṃ nārabhati. Appattassāti jhānavipassanāmaggaphaladhammassa appattassa pattiyā. Anadhigatassāti tasseva anadhigatassa adhigamatthāya. Asacchikatassāti tadeva asacchikatassa sacchikaraṇatthāya. Idaṃ paṭhamanti idaṃ “handāhaṃ nipajjāmī”ti evaṃ osīdanaṃ paṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu. Iminā nayena sabbattha attho veditabbo. Māsācitakaṃ maññeti ettha pana māsācitaṃ nāma tintamāso. Yathā tintamāso garuko hoti, evaṃ garukoti adhippāyo[V 3.0251]. Gilānā vuṭṭhito hotīti gilāno hutvā pacchā vuṭṭhito hoti. Ārambhavatthūnīti vīriyakāraṇāni. Tesampi imināva nayena attho veditabbo. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

80. In the tenth Sutta, 'grounds for laziness' means the foundations or reasons for the laziness of an indolent person; the meaning is causes of sloth. 'Work is to be done' refers to tasks such as managing robes and so forth. 'He does not arouse energy' means he does not arouse either of the two kinds of energy. 'For the unattained' means for the attainment of what has not been attained, namely, the Dhamma of jhāna, insight, path, and fruition. 'For the unachieved' means for the achievement of that very thing which has not been achieved. 'For the unrealized' means for the realization of that very thing which has not been realized. 'This is the first' refers to this sinking down, thinking, “Come now, I will lie down,” as the first ground for laziness. The meaning should be understood in this way everywhere. In the phrase `Māsācitakaṃ maññe`, `māsācitaṃ` means a soaked bean. Just as a soaked bean is heavy, so too is one heavy; this is the intention. 'Having recovered from illness' means having been ill and then having recovered afterwards. 'Grounds for exertion' means reasons for energy. Their meaning should also be understood in this same way. The rest is clear everywhere.

Yamakavaggo aṭṭhamo.

The Chapter on Pairs, the Eighth.

(9) 4. Sativaggo

(9) 4. The Chapter on Mindfulness

1-2. Satisampajaññasuttavaṇṇanā

1-2. The Commentary on the Discourse on Mindfulness and Clear Comprehension

81-82. Navamassa paṭhamaṃ heṭṭhā vuttanayameva. Dutiye saddhoti duvidhāya saddhāya samannāgato. No cupasaṅkamitāti na upaṭṭhahati. No [M 3.0253] ca paripucchitāti atthānatthaṃ kāraṇākāraṇaṃ paripucchitā na hoti. Samannāgatoti [P 4.0158] sāmiatthe paccattaṃ, samannāgatassāti vuttaṃ hoti. Ekantapaṭibhānā tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā hotīti tathāgatassa ekantapaṭibhānā dhammadesanā hoti, ekanteneva paṭibhāti upaṭṭhātīti attho.

81-82. The first of the ninth chapter is just as explained previously. In the second Sutta, 'faithful' means endowed with the two kinds of faith. 'Does not approach' means does not attend upon. 'And does not inquire' means is not one who asks about what is beneficial and unbeneficial, what is a cause and a non-cause. The word `samannāgataṃ` is in the nominative case in the sense of ownership; it is said to mean 'of one who is endowed'. 'The Tathāgata’s teaching is entirely spontaneous' means the Tathāgata’s teaching is entirely apparent; the meaning is that it appears, it presents itself, with complete certainty.

3. Mūlakasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Explanation of the Root Discourse

83. Tatiye sabbe dhammāti pañcakkhandhā. Chandamūlakāti ajjhāsayacchando kattukamyatāchando taṃ mūlaṃ etesanti chandamūlakā. Manasikārato sambhavantīti manasikārasambhavā. Phassato samudenti rāsī bhavantīti phassasamudayā. Vedanāya samosarantīti vedanāsamosaraṇā. Samādhi etesaṃ pamukhoti samādhippamukhā. Jeṭṭhakaṭṭhena sati adhipati etesanti satādhipateyyā, satijeṭṭhakāti attho. Paññā uttarā etesanti paññuttarā. Vimutti eva sāro etesanti vimuttisārā. Ettha ca chandamūlakādayo cattāropi lokiyā kathitā, sesā lokiyalokuttaramissakāti.

83. In the third, 'all phenomena' means the five aggregates. 'Rooted in desire' (chandamūlakā) means the desire of inclination and the desire to act; that is their root, therefore they are called 'rooted in desire'. 'Originating from attention' (manasikārasambhavā) means they originate from attention. 'Arising from contact' (phassasamudayā) means they arise from contact and become a mass. 'Converging on feeling' (vedanāsamosaraṇā) means they converge in feeling. 'Concentration is their chief' (samādhippamukhā) means concentration is foremost among them. 'Mindfulness is their sovereign' (satādhipateyyā) means mindfulness is their ruler in the sense of being the leader; the meaning is they have mindfulness as their leader. 'Wisdom is their supreme' (paññuttarā) means wisdom is supreme for them. 'Liberation is their essence' (vimuttisārā) means liberation alone is their essence. And here, the first four—rooted in desire and so on—are said to be worldly, while the remaining are a mixture of worldly and supramundane.

4. Corasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Explanation of the Discourse on Thieves

84. Catutthe [V 3.0252] mahācoroti rajjantare dubbhituṃ samattho mahācoro. Pariyāpajjatīti pariyādānaṃ gacchati. Na ciraṭṭhitiko hotīti addhānaṃ pālento ṭhātuṃ na sakkoti. Appaharantassa paharatīti attano averine appaharante guṇasampanne ca mahallake ca taruṇadārake ca appaharitabbayuttake paharati. Anavasesaṃ ādiyatīti nissesaṃ gaṇhāti. Byattacorānañhi idaṃ vattaṃ – parassa dvīsu sāṭakesu eko gahetabbo, ekasmiṃ sante dubbalaṃ datvā thiro gahetabbo. Puṭabhattataṇḍulādīsu ekaṃ koṭṭhāsaṃ datvā eko gahetabboti. Accāsanne kammaṃ karotīti gāmanigamarājadhānīnaṃ āsannaṭṭhāne corikakammaṃ karoti. Na ca nidhānakusalo hotīti yaṃ laddhaṃ, taṃ dakkhiṇeyye nidahituṃ cheko na hoti, paralokamaggaṃ na sodheti.

84. In the fourth, the great thief (mahācoro) is a great thief who is capable of rebelling within the kingdom. 'He is consumed' (pariyāpajjatīti) means he comes to an end. 'He does not endure for long' (na ciraṭṭhitiko hoti) means he is not able to endure for a long time while on his guard. 'He strikes those who do not strike him' (appaharantassa paharati) means he strikes his non-enemies who do not strike, and also those who should not be struck: the virtuous, the elderly, and young children. 'He takes without remainder' (anavasesaṃ ādiyati) means he takes everything without remainder. Indeed, this is the practice of skilled thieves: of another's two garments, one should be taken; if there is only one, a weak one should be given and a strong one taken. Of provisions such as packed rice and uncooked rice, one portion should be given and one taken. 'He acts very close by' (accāsanne kammaṃ karoti) means he commits thievery near villages, towns, and royal capitals. 'And he is not skilled in storing' (na ca nidhānakusalo hoti) means he is not skilled in depositing what he has gained with those worthy of offerings; he does not purify the path to the next world.

5. Samaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Explanation of the Samaṇa Sutta

85. Pañcame [M 3.0254][P 4.0159] yaṃ samaṇenāti yaṃ guṇajātaṃ samaṇena pattabbaṃ. Vusīmatāti brahmacariyavāsaṃvutena. Mutto mocemi bandhanāti ahaṃ sabbabandhanehi mutto hutvā mahājanampi rāgādibandhanato mocemi. Paramadantoti aññena kenaci asikkhāpito acodito sayambhuñāṇena paṭivijjhitvā paramadamathena dantattā paramadanto nāma. Parinibbutoti kilesaparinibbānena parinibbuto.

85. In the fifth, 'what by an ascetic' (yaṃ samaṇena) means the collection of qualities that should be attained by an ascetic. 'One who has lived the holy life' (vusīmatā) means one who has practiced the dwelling in the holy life. 'Freed, I free from bonds' (mutto mocemi bandhanā) means: I, being freed from all bonds, also free the great multitude from the bonds of lust and so forth. 'Supremely tamed' (paramadanto) is so called because, not having been taught or urged by anyone else, he has penetrated the truth with self-originated knowledge and is thus tamed by the supreme taming. 'Fully extinguished' (parinibbuto) means fully extinguished by the extinguishing of defilements.

6. Yasasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Explanation of the Yasa Sutta

86. Chaṭṭhe mā ca mayā yasoti yaso ca mayā saddhiṃ mā gañchi. Akasiralābhīti vipulalābhī. Sīlapaññāṇanti sīlañceva ñāṇañca. Saṅgammāti sannipatitvā. Samāgammāti samāgantvā. Saṅgaṇikavihāranti gaṇasaṅgaṇikavihāraṃ. Na hi nūnameti na hi nūna ime. Tathā hi panameti tathā hi pana ime. Aṅgulipatodakehīti aṅgulipatodayaṭṭhiṃ katvā vijjhanena. Sañjagghanteti mahāhasitaṃ hasante. Saṃkīḷanteti keḷiṃ karonte.

86. In the sixth, 'and may there be no retinue with me' (mā ca mayā yaso) means may a retinue not go with me. 'One who gains without difficulty' (akasiralābhī) means one who gains abundantly. 'Virtue and wisdom' (sīlapaññāṇaṃ) means virtue and wisdom. 'Having come together' (saṅgammā) means having assembled. 'Having met' (samāgammā) means having come together. 'Dwelling in company' (saṅgaṇikavihāraṃ) means dwelling in association with a group. The phrase 'na hi nūnameti' is to be parsed as 'na hi nūna ime'. The phrase 'tathā hi panameti' is to be parsed as 'tathā hi pana ime'. 'With finger-goads' (aṅgulipatodakehī) means by piercing with a finger used as a goad. 'Laughing loudly' (sañjagghante) means bursting into loud laughter. 'Playing' (saṃkīḷante) means playing.

7. Pattanikujjanasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Pattanikujjana Sutta

87. Sattame [V 3.0253] nikkujjeyyāti tena dinnassa deyyadhammassa appaṭiggahaṇatthaṃ pattanikkujjanakammavācāya nikujjeyya, na adhomukhaṭhapanena. Alābhāyāti [P 4.0160] catunnaṃ paccayānaṃ alābhatthāya. Anatthāyāti upaddavāya avaḍḍhiyā. Ukkujjeyyāti ukkujjanakammavācāya ukkujjeyya.

87. In the seventh, `nikkujjeyyāti` means one should overturn the bowl with the formal act of overturning the bowl (pattanikkujjanakammavācā) for the purpose of not accepting the offered requisite, not by placing it with its mouth facing downwards. `Alābhāyāti` means for the non-receipt of the four requisites. `Anatthāyāti` means for harm, for non-prosperity. `Ukkujjeyyāti` means one should turn the bowl upright with the formal act of turning the bowl upright.

8. Appasādapavedanīyasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Appasādapavedanīya Sutta

88. Aṭṭhame appasādaṃ pavedeyyunti appasannabhāvaṃ jānāpeyyuṃ. Appasādaṃ pavedentena pana kiṃ kātabbanti? Nisinnāsanato na uṭṭhātabbaṃ na vanditabbaṃ na paccuggamanaṃ kātabbaṃ, na deyyadhammo dātabbo. Agocareti pañcavidhe agocare.

88. In the eighth, `appasādaṃ pavedeyyunti` means they should make known the state of being unworthy of veneration. But what should be done by one who makes known this lack of veneration? One should not rise from one's seat, one should not pay homage, one should not go out to meet them, and a gift should not be given. `Agocareti` means in the five kinds of unsuitable resort.

9. Paṭisāraṇīyasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Paṭisāraṇīya Sutta

89. Navame [M 3.0255] dhammikañca gihipaṭissavanti “imaṃ temāsaṃ idheva vasitabba”nti vutto “evaṃ hotū”tiādinā nayena paṭissavaṃ. Na saccāpetīti vuttaṃ na saccaṃ karoti visaṃvādeti.

89. In the ninth, `dhammikañca gihipaṭissavaṃ` is a righteous promise to a householder, by the method of replying, “So be it,” and so on, when one has been told, “You must stay right here for these three months.” `Na saccāpetīti` means one does not make it true; one breaks one's promise.

10. Sammāvattanasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Sammāvattana Sutta

90. Dasame paccekaṭṭhāneti adhipatiṭṭhāne jeṭṭhakaṭṭhāne. Tañhi jeṭṭhakaṃ katvā kiñci saṅghakammaṃ kātuṃ na labhati. Na ca tena mūlena vuṭṭhāpetabboti taṃ mūlaṃ katvā abbhānakammaṃ kātuṃ na labhati. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

90. In the tenth, `paccekaṭṭhāne` means in a position of authority, in the position of a chief. For having made him the chief, one is not allowed to perform any Saṅgha act. And he is not to be rehabilitated by that as the root, meaning one is not allowed to perform the act of rehabilitation by making that the root. The rest is clear in all cases.

Sativaggo navamo.

The Chapter on Mindfulness is the ninth.

(10) 5. Sāmaññavaggo

(10) 5. The Section on the State of an Ascetic

91. Ito [V 3.0254] paraṃ atha kho bojjhā upāsikātiādīsu bojjhā upāsikā, sirimā upāsikā, padumā upāsikā, sutanā upāsikā, manujā upāsikā, uttarā upāsikā, muttā upāsikā, khemā upāsikā, rucī upāsikā, cundī rājakumārī, bimbī upāsikā, sumanā rājakumārī, mallikā devī[P 4.0161], tissā upāsikā, tissāmātā upāsikā, soṇā upāsikā, soṇāya mātā upāsikā, kāṇā upāsikā, kāṇamātā upāsikā, uttarā nandamātā, visākhā migāramātā, khujjuttarā upāsikā, sāmāvatī upāsikā, suppavāsā koliyadhītā, suppiyā upāsikā, nakulamātā gahapatānīti imāsaṃ ettakānaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathakammameva kathitaṃ. Icchantena vitthāretvā kathetabbaṃ. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānatthamevāti.

91. From here onward, in the suttas beginning with “Atha kho bojjhā upāsikā,” for these many—Bojjhā the female lay supporter, Sirimā the female lay supporter, Padumā the female lay supporter, Sutanā the female lay supporter, Manujā the female lay supporter, Uttarā the female lay supporter, Muttā the female lay supporter, Khemā the female lay supporter, Rucī the female lay supporter, Cundī the princess, Bimbī the female lay supporter, Sumanā the princess, Mallikā the queen, Tissā the female lay supporter, Tissāmātā the female lay supporter, Soṇā the female lay supporter, Soṇā’s mother the female lay supporter, Kāṇā the female lay supporter, Kāṇā’s mother the female lay supporter, Uttarā Nandamātā, Visākhā Migāramātā, Khujjuttarā the female lay supporter, Sāmāvatī the female lay supporter, Suppavāsā the Koliyan daughter, Suppiyā the female lay supporter, and Nakulamātā the householder’s wife—only the Uposatha act endowed with eight factors has been taught. One who wishes may elaborate and explain it. The rest has a clear meaning in all instances.

Manorathapūraṇiyā aṅguttaranikāya-aṭṭhakathāya

In the Manorathapūraṇī, the commentary to the Aṅguttara Nikāya,

Aṭṭhakanipātassa saṃvaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Book of the Eights is finished.

. Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Homage to that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Aṅguttaranikāye

In the Aṅguttara Nikāya

Navakanipāta-aṭṭhakathā

Commentary on the Book of the Nines

1. Paṭhamapaṇṇāsakaṃ

1. The First Fifty

1. Sambodhivaggo

1. The Chapter on Enlightenment

1. Sambodhisuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on Enlightenment

1. Navakanipātassa [V 3.0255][M 3.0257][P 4.0162] paṭhame sambodhipakkhikānanti catumaggasaṅkhātassa sambodhissa pakkhe bhavānaṃ, upakārakānanti attho. Pāḷiyaṃ āgate nava dhamme sandhāyevaṃ pucchati. Kā upanisāti ko upanissayapaccayo. Abhisallekhantīti abhisallekhikā. Samathavipassanācittassa vivaraṇe sappāyā upakārakāti cetovivaraṇasappāyā. Appicchataṃ ārabbha pavattā kathā appicchakathā. Sesesupi eseva nayo.

1. In the first sutta of the Navakanipāta, `sambodhipakkhikānaṃ` means being on the side of enlightenment—which is designated as the four paths—and being beneficial. Referring to the nine dhammas that come in the Pāḷi, He asks thus. `Kā upanisā` means: what is the strong supporting condition? Because they wear away defilements, they are called `abhisallekhikā`. Because they are suitable and helpful for the opening of the mind of serenity and insight, they are called `cetovivaraṇasappāyā`. Talk that arises in connection with having few wishes is called `appicchakathā`. This same method applies to the remaining terms.

Asubhā [V 3.0256] bhāvetabbā rāgassa pahānāyāti ayamattho sālilāyakopamāya vibhāvetabbo – eko hi puriso asitaṃ gahetvā koṭito paṭṭhāya sālikkhette sāliyo lāyati. Athassa vatiṃ bhinditvā gāvo pavisiṃsu. So asitaṃ ṭhapetvā yaṭṭhiṃ ādāya teneva maggena gāvo nīharitvā vatiṃ pākatikaṃ katvā punapi asitaṃ ādāya sāliyo lāyi. Ettha sālikkhettaṃ viya buddhasāsanaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ, sālilāyako viya yogāvacaro, asitaṃ viya paññā, lāyanakālo viya vipassanāya kammakaraṇakālo, yaṭṭhi viya asubhakammaṭṭhānaṃ, vati viya saṃvaro, vatiṃ bhinditvā gāvīnaṃ [P 4.0163] pavisanaṃ viya sahasā appaṭisaṅkhāya pamādaṃ ārabbha rāgassa uppajjanaṃ, asitaṃ ṭhapetvā yaṭṭhiṃ ādāya paviṭṭhamaggeneva gāvo nīharitvā vatiṃ paṭipākatikaṃ katvā puna koṭito paṭṭhāya sālilāyanaṃ viya asubhakammaṭṭhānena rāgaṃ vikkhambhetvā puna vipassanāya kammaṃ ārabhanakālo. Imamatthaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ – “asubhā bhāvetabbā rāgassa pahānāyā”ti.

“The contemplation of the repulsive should be cultivated for the abandonment of lust”—this meaning should be made clear through the simile of the rice reaper. A certain man, taking a sickle, begins to reap rice from the edge of a rice field. Then, breaking through his fence, cows entered. He, having put down the sickle and taken a staff, drove out the cows by the same path they had entered, made the fence as it was before, and again taking the sickle, reaped the rice. Herein, the Buddha’s dispensation should be seen like the rice field; the yogī practitioner like the rice reaper; wisdom like the sickle; the time of undertaking the meditation practice in insight like the time of reaping; the meditation on foulness like the staff; restraint like the fence; the arising of lust due to heedlessness, without reflection, suddenly, like the entering of the cows after breaking the fence; and the time of again undertaking the meditation practice in insight after having suppressed lust by means of the meditation on foulness, like putting down the sickle, taking a staff, driving out the cows by the same path they had entered, making the fence as it was before, and again reaping the rice starting from the edge. Referring to this meaning, it was said: “The contemplation of the repulsive should be cultivated for the abandonment of lust.”

Tattha [M 3.0258] rāgassāti pañcakāmaguṇikarāgassa. Mettāti mettākammaṭṭhānaṃ. Byāpādassa pahānāyāti vuttanayeneva uppannassa kopassa pajahanatthāya. Ānāpānassatīti soḷasavatthukā ānāpānassati. Vitakkupacchedāyāti vuttanayeneva uppannānaṃ vitakkānaṃ upacchedanatthāya. Asmimānasamugghātāyāti asmīti uppajjanakassa mānassa samugghātatthāya. Anattasaññā saṇṭhātīti aniccalakkhaṇe diṭṭhe anattalakkhaṇaṃ diṭṭhameva hoti. Etesu hi tīsu lakkhaṇesu ekasmiṃ diṭṭhe itaradvayaṃ diṭṭhameva hoti. Tena vuttaṃ – “aniccasaññino, bhikkhave, anattasaññā saṇṭhātī”ti. Diṭṭheva dhamme nibbānanti diṭṭheyeva dhamme apaccayaparinibbānañca pāpuṇātīti imasmiṃ sutte vaṭṭavivaṭṭaṃ kathitaṃ.

Herein, 'of lust' refers to the lust for the five strands of sensual pleasure. 'Loving-kindness' refers to the meditation practice of loving-kindness. 'For the abandoning of ill will' is for the purpose of abandoning arisen anger, in accordance with the method already stated. 'Mindfulness of breathing' refers to the mindfulness of breathing with its sixteen bases. 'For the cutting off of thoughts' is for the purpose of cutting off arisen thoughts, in accordance with the method already stated. 'For the uprooting of the conceit "I am"' is for the purpose of uprooting the conceit that arises as "I am." 'The perception of not-self becomes established' means that when the characteristic of impermanence is seen, the characteristic of not-self is also seen. For among these three characteristics, when one is seen, the other two are also seen. Therefore, it was said: “Bhikkhus, for one who perceives impermanence, the perception of not-self becomes established.” 'Nibbāna in this very life' means that in this very life one attains final Nibbāna without remaining condition. In this discourse, the round of existence and the escape from the round have been taught.

2. Nissayasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Discourse on Dependence

2. Dutiye nissayasampannoti patiṭṭhāsampanno. Saddhanti okappanasaddhaṃ. Vīriyanti kāyikacetasikavīriyaṃ. Yaṃsāti yaṃ assa. Ariyāya [P 4.0164] paññāyāti sahavipassanāya maggapaññāya. Saṅkhāyāti jānitvā. Ekaṃ paṭisevatīti sevitabbayuttakaṃ sevati. Adhivāsetīti adhivāsetabbayuttakaṃ adhivāseti. Parivajjetīti parivajjetabbayuttakaṃ parivajjeti. Vinodetīti nīharitabbayuttakaṃ nīharati. Evaṃ kho bhikkhūti evaṃ kho bhikkhu uggahaparipucchāvasena ceva dhammavavatthānavasena [V 3.0257] ca paṭisevitabbādīni suppaṭividdhāni supaccakkhāni katvā paṭisevanto adhivāsento parivajjento vinodento ca bhikkhu nissayasampanno nāma hotīti.

2. In the second [discourse], 'endowed with dependence' (nissayasampanno) means 'endowed with a basis' (patiṭṭhānasampanno). 'Faith' (saddhā) means 'conviction' (okappanasaddhā). 'Energy' (vīriya) means 'bodily and mental energy' (kāyikacetasikavīriya). 'Yaṃsāti' is to be divided as 'yaṃ assa' (whatever is for one). 'With noble wisdom' (ariyāya paññāya) means 'with the wisdom of the path together with insight' (sahavipassanāya maggapaññāya). 'Having discerned' (saṅkhāya) means 'having known' (jānitvā). 'He makes use of one thing' (ekaṃ paṭisevati) means 'he makes use of what is suitable to be made use of' (sevitabbayuttakaṃ sevati). 'He endures' (adhivāseti) means 'he endures what is suitable to be endured' (adhivāsetabbayuttakaṃ adhivāseti). 'He avoids' (parivajjeti) means 'he avoids what is suitable to be avoided' (parivajjetabbayuttakaṃ parivajjeti). 'He dispels' (vinodeti) means 'he removes what is suitable to be removed' (nīharitabbayuttakaṃ nīharati). 'Thus, bhikkhu' (evaṃ kho bhikkhu)—thus, a bhikkhu, having thoroughly penetrated (suppaṭividdhāni) and clearly realized (supaccakkhāni) what should be made use of and so forth (paṭisevitabbādīni) by means of learning and questioning (uggahaparipucchāvasena) and by means of determining phenomena (dhammavavatthānavasena), who makes use of, endures, avoids, and dispels, is called 'endowed with dependence' (nissayasampanno).

3. Meghiyasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Discourse to Meghiya

3. Tatiye cālikāyanti evaṃnāmake nagare. Taṃ kira calamaggaṃ nissāya katattā olokentānaṃ calamānaṃ viya upaṭṭhāti, tasmā cālikāti saṅkhaṃ gataṃ. Cāliyapabbateti sopi pabbato sabbasetattā kāḷapakkhuposathe olokentānaṃ calamāno viya upaṭṭhāti, tasmā cāliyapabbatoti vutto. Tattha mahantaṃ vihāraṃ kārayiṃsu. Iti bhagavā taṃ nagaraṃ nissāya cālikāpabbatamahāvihāre viharati. Jantugāmanti evaṃnāmakaṃ aparampi tasseva vihārassa gocaragāmaṃ. Jattugāmantipi [M 3.0259] paṭhanti. Padhānatthikassāti padhānakammikassa. Padhānāyāti samaṇadhammakaraṇatthāya. Āgamehi tāvāti satthā therassa vacanaṃ sutvā upadhārento “na tāvassa ñāṇaṃ paripakka”nti ñatvā paṭibāhanto evamāha. Ekakamhi tāvāti idaṃ panassa “evamayaṃ gantvāpi [P 4.0165] kamme anipphajjamāne nirāsaṅko hutvā pemavasena puna āgacchissatī”ti cittamaddavajananatthaṃ āha. Natthi kiñci uttari karaṇīyanti catūsu saccesu catunnaṃ kiccānaṃ katattā aññaṃ uttari karaṇīyaṃ nāma natthi. Katassa vā paṭicayoti adhigatassa vā puna paṭicayopi natthi. Na hi bhāvitamaggo puna bhāvīyati, na pahīnakilesānaṃ puna pahānaṃ atthi. Padhānanti kho, meghiya, vadamānaṃ kinti vadeyyāmāti “samaṇadhammaṃ karomī”ti taṃ vadamānaṃ mayaṃ aññaṃ kiṃ nāma vadeyyāma.

3. In the third [discourse], 'at Cālikā' (cālikāya) means 'in a city of that name' (evaṃnāmake nagare). It is said that because it was built relying on a moving fire, it appeared to those looking (olokentānaṃ) as if it were swaying (calamānaṃ viya upaṭṭhāti); therefore, it came to be known as Cālikā (cālikāti saṅkhaṃ gataṃ). 'Mount Cāliya' (cāliyapabbate)—that mountain too, because it was entirely white (sabbasetattā), appeared to those looking at it on the dark-moon observance day (kāḷapakkhuposathe olokentānaṃ) as if it were swaying (calamāno viya upaṭṭhāti); therefore, it was called Mount Cāliya (cāliyapabbatoti vutto). There, they built a large monastery. Thus, the Blessed One was dwelling in the great monastery on Mount Cālikā, relying on that city. 'Jantugāma' (jantugāmaṃ)—another alms-resort village of that very monastery, with that name (evaṃnāmakaṃ aparampi tasseva vihārassa gocaragāmaṃ). Some also read it as 'Jattugāma' (jattugāmantipi paṭhanti). 'For one desiring to strive' (padhānatthikassa) means 'for one who undertakes the practice of striving' (padhānakammikassa). 'For striving' (padhānāya) means 'for the purpose of practicing the ascetic's duty' (samaṇadhammakaraṇatthāya). As for 'Wait a while' (āgamehi tāva), the Teacher, having heard the elder's words and investigating, and knowing, 'His knowledge is not yet mature,' and wishing to forbid him, spoke thus. As for 'since I am alone' (ekakamhi tāva), he said this to generate softness in his mind, thinking, 'Thus, even if he goes and the task is not accomplished, he will return again out of affection, being free from apprehension.' 'There is nothing further to be done' (natthi kiñci uttari karaṇīyaṃ)—because the four tasks regarding the four truths have been accomplished, there is nothing else further to be done (aññaṃ uttari karaṇīyaṃ nāma natthi). 'Or accumulation for what has been done' (katassa vā paṭicayo)—for what has been attained, there is no further accumulation (adhigatassa vā puna paṭicayopi natthi). Indeed, the developed path is not developed again, nor is there any further abandoning for defilements that have been abandoned. 'When one speaks of striving, Meghiya, what should we say?' (padhānanti kho, meghiya, vadamānaṃ kinti vadeyyāma)—to you who say, 'I will practice the ascetic's duty' (samaṇadhammaṃ karomīti), what else should we say?

Divāvihāraṃ nisīdīti divāvihāratthāya nisīdi. Nisīdanto ca yasmiṃ maṅgalasilāpaṭṭe pubbe anupaṭipāṭiyā pañca jātisatāni rājā hutvā uyyānakīḷikaṃ kīḷanto tividhanāṭakaparivāro nisīdi, tasmiṃyeva nisīdi. Athassa nisinnakālato paṭṭhāya samaṇabhāvo jahito viya ahosi, rājavesaṃ gahetvā nāṭakavaraparivuto setacchattassa heṭṭhā mahārahe pallaṅke nisinno viya jāto. Athassa taṃ sampattiṃ assādayato kāmavitakko udapādi. So tasmiṃyeva khaṇe mahāyodhehi gahite dve core ānetvā purato ṭhapite viya addasa. Tesu ekassa vadhaṃ āṇāpanavasenassa byāpādavitakko uppajji, ekassa bandhanaṃ āṇāpanavasena vihiṃsāvitakko. Evaṃ so latājālena rukkho viya madhumakkhikāhi madhughātako viya akusalavitakkehi [V 3.0258][P 4.0166] parikkhitto ahosi. Taṃ sandhāya – atha kho āyasmato meghiyassātiādi vuttaṃ. Anvāsattāti anubaddhā samparivāritā. Yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamīti evaṃ pāpavitakkehi samparikiṇṇo kammaṭṭhānaṃ sappāyaṃ kātuṃ asakkonto “idaṃ vata disvā dīghadassī bhagavā paṭisedhesī”ti sallakkhetvā “idaṃ kāraṇaṃ dasabalassa ārocessāmī”ti nisinnāsanato vuṭṭhāya yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami.

'He sat down for the day's abiding' (divāvihāraṃ nisīdi) means 'he sat down for the purpose of the day's abiding' (divāvihāratthāya nisīdi). And while sitting, he sat right there on the auspicious stone slab (maṅgalasilāpaṭṭe) where, previously, in a succession (anupaṭipāṭiyā) of five hundred births (pañca jātisatāni), he had been a king (rājā hutvā) and, while enjoying the pleasures of the park (uyyānakīḷikaṃ kīḷanto), had sat surrounded by a threefold theatrical troupe (tividhanāṭakaparivāro). Then, from the moment he sat down (nisinnakālato paṭṭhāya), his state as a recluse (samaṇabhāvo) seemed abandoned (jahito viya ahosi); he became as if he had taken on the attire of a king (rājavesaṃ gahetvā), surrounded by the finest actors (nāṭakavaraparivuto), seated beneath a white parasol (setacchattassa heṭṭhā) on a couch worthy of great men (mahārahe pallaṅke). Then, as he was savoring that prosperity (taṃ sampattiṃ assādayato), a thought of sensuality (kāmavitakko) arose in him (udapādi). At that very moment (tasmiṃyeva khaṇe), he saw as if two thieves (dve core), captured by great warriors (mahāyodhehi gahite), were brought and placed before him (ānetvā purato ṭhapite viya addasa). In him, by way of ordering the killing of one of them, a thought of ill will (byāpādavitakko) arose; by way of ordering the binding of the other, a thought of cruelty (vihiṃsāvitakko) arose. Thus, like a tree entwined by a net of creepers (latājālena rukkho viya), like a honey-destroyer surrounded by bees (madhumakkhikāhi madhughātako viya), he was surrounded by unwholesome thoughts. With reference to this, it is said: 'Then, for the Venerable Meghiya...' (atha kho āyasmato meghiyassātiādi). 'Beset' (anvāsattā) means 'pursued' (anubaddhā) and 'surrounded' (samparivāritā). 'He approached the Blessed One' (yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami)—thus, being pervaded by evil thoughts (pāpavitakkehi samparikiṇṇo) and unable to practice a suitable meditation subject (kammaṭṭhānaṃ sappāyaṃ kātuṃ asakkonto), he considered, 'Indeed, it was seeing this that the far-seeing Blessed One forbade me!' (idaṃ vata disvā dīghadassī bhagavā paṭisedhesīti), and thinking, 'I shall report this matter to the One of Ten Powers' (idaṃ kāraṇaṃ dasabalassa ārocessāmīti), he rose from his seat (nisinnāsanato vuṭṭhāya) and approached the Blessed One (yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami).

4. Nandakasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Nandaka Sutta

4. Catutthe [M 3.0260] upaṭṭhānasālāyanti bhojanasālāyaṃ. Yenupaṭṭhānasālāti satthā nandakattherena madhurassarena āraddhāya dhammadesanāya saddaṃ sutvā, “ānanda, ko eso upaṭṭhānasālāya madhurassarena dhammaṃ desetī”ti pucchitvā “dhammakathikanandakattherassa ajja, bhante, vāro”ti sutvā “atimadhuraṃ katvā, ānanda, eso bhikkhu dhammaṃ katheti, mayampi gantvā suṇissāmā”ti vatvā yenupaṭṭhānasālā tenupasaṅkami. Bahidvārakoṭṭhake aṭṭhāsīti chabbaṇṇarasmiyo cīvaragabbhe paṭicchādetvā aññātakavesena aṭṭhāsi. Kathāpariyosānaṃ āgamayamānoti “idamavocā”ti idaṃ kathāvasānaṃ udikkhamāno dhammakathaṃ suṇanto aṭṭhāsiyeva. Athāyasmā ānando nikkhante paṭhame yāme satthu saññaṃ [P 4.0167] adāsi – “paṭhamayāmo atikkanto, bhante, thokaṃ vissamathā”ti. Satthā tattheva aṭṭhāsi. Athāyasmā ānando majjhimayāmepi nikkhante, “bhante, tumhe pakatiyā khattiyasukhumālā, puna buddhasukhumālāti paramasukhumālā, majjhimayāmopi atikkanto, muhuttaṃ vissamathā”ti āha. Satthā tattheva aṭṭhāsi. Tattha ṭhitakassevassa aruṇaggaṃ paññāyittha. Aruṇuggamanañca therassa “idamavocā”ti pāpetvā kathāpariyosānañca dasabalassa chabbaṇṇasarīrasmivissajjanañca ekappahāreneva ahosi. Aggaḷaṃ ākoṭesīti agganakhena dvārakavāṭaṃ ākoṭesi.

4. In the fourth [sutta], 'in the assembly hall' means in the refectory. Regarding the phrase 'where the assembly hall was,' the Teacher, hearing the sound of the Dhamma talk initiated by the Elder Nandaka with a sweet voice, asked, 'Ānanda, who is that in the assembly hall delivering the Dhamma with such a sweet voice?' Upon hearing, 'Venerable sir, today is the turn of the Dhamma speaker, the Elder Nandaka,' he said, 'Ānanda, this monk speaks the Dhamma most sweetly. Let us also go and listen.' So saying, he approached the assembly hall. 'He stood outside the door frame' means he concealed his six-colored rays within his robe and stood there in an unrecognized guise. 'Awaiting the conclusion of the talk' means, expecting the end of the talk, that is, 'This was said,' he stood there listening to the Dhamma talk. Then, when the first watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda gave a sign to the Teacher: 'Venerable sir, the first watch has passed, please rest a little.' The Teacher remained standing right there. Then, when the middle watch had also passed, the Venerable Ānanda said, 'Venerable sir, you are by nature delicate as a noble, and again delicate as a Buddha—thus supremely delicate. The middle watch has also passed, please rest a moment.' The Teacher still stood there. While he was standing there, the first light of dawn appeared to him. The rising of dawn, the elder’s reaching 'This was said' and thus the conclusion of the talk, and the Ten-Powered One's emission of the six-colored rays from his body all happened at the very same instant. 'He knocked on the door-bolt' means he knocked on the door-leaf with the tip of his fingernail.

Sārajjamānarūpoti harāyamāno ottappamāno. Domanassasārajjaṃ panassa natthi. Ettakampi no nappaṭibhāseyyāti paṭisambhidāppattassa appaṭibhānaṃ nāma natthi. Ettakampi na katheyyanti dasseti. Sādhu sādhūti therassa dhammadesanaṃ sampahaṃsanto āha. Ayañhettha attho “sugahitā ca te dhammadesanā sukathitā cā”ti. Kulaputtānanti ācārakulaputtānañceva jātikulaputtānañca. Ariyo ca tuṇhibhāvoti dutiyajjhānasamāpattiṃ sandhāyevamāha. Adhipaññādhammavipassanāyāti saṅkhārapariggahavipassanāñāṇassa[V 3.0259]. Catuppādakoti [P 4.0168] assagoṇagadrabhādiko. Idaṃ vatvāti imaṃ catūhaṅgehi samannāgataṃ dhammaṃ kathayitvā. Vihāraṃ pāvisīti gandhakuṭiṃ paviṭṭho.

'Appearing abashed' means being ashamed and having moral dread. However, he has no abashment born of dejection. Regarding 'Would even this much not occur to me?': for one who has attained the analytical knowledges, there is no such thing as a lack of inspiration. This shows the meaning: 'I would not speak even this much.' 'Excellent, excellent!' he said, delighting in the elder's Dhamma discourse. Herein, this is the meaning: 'Your Dhamma discourse is well-grasped and well-spoken.' 'Sons of good families' means both sons of good families by conduct and sons of good families by birth. 'And the noble silence'—this he said referring only to the attainment of the second jhāna. 'Through the higher wisdom of insight into the Dhamma' refers to the insight-knowledge that comprehends conditioned phenomena. 'Four-footed' means such as horses, cattle, and donkeys. 'Having said this' means having spoken this Dhamma endowed with four factors. 'He entered the dwelling' means he entered the perfumed chamber.

Kālena dhammassavaneti kāle kāle dhammassavanasmiṃ. Dhammasākacchāyāti pañhakathāya. Gambhīraṃ atthapadanti gambhīraṃ guḷhaṃ rahassaṃ atthaṃ. Paññāyāti [M 3.0261] sahavipassanāya maggapaññāya. Sammasanapaṭivedhapaññāpi uggahaparipucchāpaññāpi vaṭṭatiyeva. Patto vā pajjati vāti arahattaṃ patto vā pāpuṇissati vāti evaṃ guṇasambhāvanāya sambhāveti. Appattamānasāti appattaarahattā, arahattaṃ vā appattaṃ mānasaṃ etesantipi appattamānasā. Diṭṭhadhammasukhavihāranti ettha diṭṭhadhammasukhavihāro lokiyopi vaṭṭati lokuttaropi.

'Timely listening to the Dhamma' means listening to the Dhamma at suitable times. 'Discussion of the Dhamma' means in discussion of questions. 'A profound state of meaning' means a profound, hidden, secret meaning. 'With wisdom' means by the wisdom of the path together with insight. The wisdom of comprehension and penetration, and also the wisdom arisen from learning and questioning, are indeed applicable. 'Has attained or will attain' means he has either attained Arahantship or he will attain it; thus he is esteemed by way of showing forth his qualities. 'With minds not yet attained' means those who have not yet attained Arahantship; or, for them there is a mind that has not attained Arahantship, therefore they are also called 'with minds not yet attained.' Regarding 'a blissful dwelling in this very life,' herein, a blissful dwelling in this very life is applicable as both worldly and supramundane.

5. Balasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Explanation of the Bala Sutta

5. Pañcame avijjākosajjasāvajjaassaddhiyesu akampanato paññābalādīni daṭṭhabbāni. Akusalasaṅkhātāti akusalāti ñātā. Esa nayo sabbattha. Nālamariyāti ariyabhāvaṃ kātuṃ asamatthā, ariyānaṃ vā ananucchavikā. Vodiṭṭhāti suṭṭhu diṭṭhā. Vocaritāti manodvāre samudācārappattā. Atthikassāti dhammadesanāya atthikassa. Ājīvikābhayanti jīvitavuttibhayaṃ. Asilokabhayanti garahābhayaṃ. Parisāsārajjabhayanti parisaṃ patvā sārajjaṃ okkamanabhayaṃ. Imasmiṃ [P 4.0169] sutte vaṭṭavivaṭṭaṃ kathitaṃ.

5. In the fifth [sutta], the strengths of wisdom and so on should be understood as unwavering in the face of ignorance, laziness, blameworthiness, and lack of faith. ‘What is termed unwholesome’ means ‘known as unwholesome.’ This method applies everywhere. ‘Not worthy of the noble ones’ means ‘incapable of bringing about a noble state, or unsuitable for the noble ones.’ ‘Well-seen’ means ‘well seen.’ ‘Well-conducted’ means ‘having reached the stage of active behavior at the mind-door.’ ‘For one who is desirous’ means ‘for one desirous of the teaching of the Dhamma.’ ‘Fear of livelihood’ means ‘fear for one’s way of living.’ ‘Fear of ill-repute’ means ‘fear of censure.’ ‘Fear of lack of confidence in assemblies’ means ‘fear of falling into timidity upon entering an assembly.’ In this sutta, the round of existence and that which is free from the round are spoken of.

6. Sevanāsuttavaṇṇanā

6. Explanation of the Sevanā Sutta

6. Chaṭṭhe jīvitaparikkhārāti jīvitasambhārā. Samudānetabbāti samāharitabbā. Kasirena samudāgacchantīti dukkhena uppajjanti. Rattibhāgaṃ vā divasabhāgaṃ vāti ettha rattibhāge ñatvā rattibhāgeyeva pakkamitabbaṃ, rattiṃ caṇḍavāḷādiparipanthe sati aruṇuggamanaṃ āgametabbaṃ. Divasabhāge ñatvā divā pakkamitabbaṃ, divā paripanthe sati sūriyatthaṅgamanaṃ āgametabbaṃ. Saṅkhāpīti sāmaññatthassa bhāvanāpāripūriāgamanaṃ jānitvā. So puggaloti padassa pana “nānubandhitabbo”ti iminā sambandho. Anāpucchāti idha pana taṃ puggalaṃ anāpucchā pakkamitabbanti attho. Api panujjamānenāti api nikkaḍḍhiyamānena. Evarūpo hi puggalo sacepi [V 3.0260] dārukalāpasataṃ vā udakaghaṭasataṃ vā vālikāghaṭasataṃ vā daṇḍaṃ āropeti, mā idha vasīti nikkaḍḍhāpeti vā, taṃ khamāpetvāpi yāvajīvaṃ so anubandhitabbova, na vijahitabbo.

6. In the sixth [sutta], ‘requisites for life’ means ‘necessities of life.’ ‘Should be gathered’ means ‘should be collected.’ ‘Arisen with difficulty’ means ‘arisen with hardship.’ ‘Either the night part or the day part’ — here, having known the night part, one should depart in the night part itself; if there are dangers such as fierce wild animals at night, one should wait for the rising of the dawn. Having known the day part, one should depart by day; if there are dangers by day, one should wait for the setting of the sun. ‘Having considered’ means ‘knowing the arrival at the fulfillment of the development of the goal of recluseship.’ As for the phrase ‘that person,’ a connection should be made with this phrase: ‘should not be followed.’ ‘Without taking leave’—here, the meaning is that one should depart without taking leave of that person. ‘Even if being driven away’ means ‘even if being forcibly expelled.’ Indeed, if such a person were to impose a penalty of a hundred bundles of wood, or a hundred pots of water, or a hundred pots of sand, or were to have one expelled, saying, ‘Do not live here,’ even having made that person pleased, he should indeed be followed for as long as life lasts; he should not be abandoned.

7. Sutavāsuttavaṇṇanā

7. Explanation of the Sutavā Sutta

7. Sattame [M 3.0262] pañca ṭhānāni ajjhācaritunti pañca kāraṇāni atikkamituṃ. Pāṇanti antamaso kunthakipillikaṃ. Adinnanti antamaso tiṇasalākampi parasantakaṃ. Theyyasaṅkhātanti theyyacittena. Sannidhikārakaṃ kāme paribhuñjitunti sannidhiṃ katvā ṭhapetvā [P 4.0170] vatthukāmakilesakāme paribhuñjituṃ abhabbo. Akappiyaṃ kāmaguṇaṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ. Buddhaṃ paccakkhātunti “na buddho aya”nti evaṃ paṭikkhipituṃ. Dhammādīsupi eseva nayo. Evaṃ tāva aṭṭhakathāya āgataṃ. Pāḷiyaṃ pana imasmiṃ sutte agatigamanāni kathitāni.

7. In the seventh sutta, ‘to transgress the five positions’ means ‘to transgress the five reasons.’ ‘Living being’ means ‘even down to an ant or a termite.’ ‘What is not given’ means ‘even a blade of grass or a stick belonging to another.’ ‘What is reckoned as theft’ means ‘with the thought of theft.’ ‘To enjoy sensual pleasures by storing them up’ means ‘one is incapable of enjoying object-based sensual pleasures and defilement-based sensual pleasures by making a store and putting them aside.’ This is said with reference to unsuitable sensual qualities. ‘To reject the Buddha’ means ‘to reject him, saying, “This is not the Buddha.”’ The same method applies to the Dhamma and so on. Thus far is what has come in the commentary. In the Pāḷi text of this sutta, however, the wrong courses of action are spoken of.

8-10. Sajjhasuttādivaṇṇanā

8-10. Explanation of the Sajjha Sutta and so forth.

8-10. Aṭṭhame buddhādīnaṃ paccakkhānaṃ kathitaṃ. Navame puthujjanena saddhiṃ gahitattā “āhuneyyā”ti vuttaṃ. Dasame gotrabhūti sotāpattimaggassa anantarapaccayena sikhāpattabalavavipassanācittena samannāgato. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānatthamevāti.

8-10. In the eighth discourse, the rejection of the Buddha and so on is spoken of. In the ninth discourse, because it is taken together with the ordinary person, it is said to be ‘worthy of offerings.’ In the tenth discourse, the ‘change-of-lineage’ is a person endowed with a strong insight-mind that has reached its peak, which serves as the proximity condition for the path of stream-entry. The rest has an obvious meaning throughout.

Sambodhavaggo paṭhamo.

The First Chapter on Awakening.

2. Sīhanādavaggo

2. The Chapter on the Lion's Roar

1. Sīhanādasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Lion's Roar Discourse

11. Dutiyassa paṭhame yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamīti “sace satthā cārikaṃ pakkamitukāmo assa[V 3.0261], imasmiṃ kāle pakkameyya. Handāhaṃ cārikaṃ gamanatthāya satthāraṃ āpucchāmī”ti cintetvā bhikkhusaṅghaparivuto upasaṅkami. Āyasmā maṃ, bhanteti so kira bhikkhu theraṃ mahatā bhikkhuparivārena gacchantaṃ disvā “ime bhikkhū tathāgataṃ pahāya sāriputtaṃ parivāretvā nikkhantā, gamanavicchedamassa karissāmī”ti aṭṭhāne kopaṃ bandhitvā evamāha. Tattha [P 4.0171] āsajjāti ghaṭṭetvā. Appaṭinissajjāti akkhamāpetvā accayaṃ adesetvā. Kismiṃ pana so kāraṇe āghātaṃ bandhīti? Therassa kira dasabalaṃ vanditvā uṭṭhāya gacchato cīvarakaṇṇo tassa sarīraṃ phusi, vāto paharītipi vadanti. Ettakena āghātaṃ bandhitvā theraṃ mahatā parivārena gacchantaṃ disvā usūyamāno “gamanavicchedamassa karissāmī”ti evamāha. Ehi tvaṃ bhikkhūti satthā [M 3.0263] tassa bhikkhuno vacanaṃ sutvā “na taṃ bhikkhu sāriputto paharīti vutte, ‘bhante, tumhe attano aggasāvakasseva pakkhaṃ vahatha, na mayha’nti mayi manopadosaṃ katvā apāye nibbatteyyā”ti ñatvā “sāriputtaṃ pakkosāpetvā imamatthaṃ pucchissāmī”ti ekaṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantetvā evamāha. Avāpuraṇaṃ ādāyāti kuñcikaṃ gahetvā. Sīhanādanti seṭṭhanādaṃ pamukhanādaṃ appaṭivattiyanādaṃ. Evaṃ dvīhi mahātherehi ārocito bhikkhusaṅgho rattiṭṭhānadivāṭṭhānāni pahāya satthu santikaṃ agamāsi. Khīyanadhammanti kathādhammaṃ.

11. In the first discourse of the second chapter, regarding the phrase 'he approached where the Blessed One was,' the meaning should be understood as follows: thinking, 'If the Teacher wishes to depart on a journey, he might depart at this time. Now, I will ask leave of the Teacher for the purpose of going on a journey,' he approached, surrounded by the community of monks. Regarding 'Venerable sir, the venerable one... me...': it is said that monk, seeing the Elder going with a great retinue of monks, conceived anger in the wrong way, thinking, 'These monks, having abandoned the Tathāgata, have departed surrounding Sāriputta. I will cause an interruption to his journey,' and spoke thus. Therein, 'āsajja' means having collided with. 'Appaṭinissajja' means without having asked for forgiveness, without having declared the offense. For what reason did he conceive resentment? It is said that as the Elder was rising and departing after paying homage to the One with Ten Powers, the corner of his robe touched that monk's body; some also say the wind from it struck him. Conceiving resentment over such a small thing, and seeing the Elder departing with a great retinue, he became envious and spoke thus, thinking, 'I will cause an interruption to his journey.' Regarding 'Come, you, monk': the Teacher, having heard that monk's words, knew, 'If it is said, "Monk, Sāriputta did not strike you," he might, having created ill will towards me by saying, "Venerable sir, you take the side of only your own chief disciple, you do not take my side," be reborn in a state of woe.' Thinking, 'I will have Sāriputta summoned and ask about this matter,' he addressed a certain monk and spoke thus. 'Avāpuraṇaṃ ādāya' means taking the key. 'Sīhanādaṃ' means the best roar, the foremost roar, the irrefutable roar. Thus informed by the two great elders, the community of monks, having abandoned their night-time and day-time places, went to the Teacher's presence. 'Khīyanadhammaṃ' means the matter of discussion.

Gūthagatanti gūthameva. Sesesupi eseva nayo. Pathavīsamenāti akujjhanaṭṭhena pathaviyā samānena. Na hi pathavī “mayi suciṃ nikkhipantī”ti somanassaṃ karoti, na “asuciṃ nikkhipantī”ti domanassaṃ. Mayhampi evarūpaṃ cittanti dasseti. Vipulenāti aparittena. Mahaggatenāti mahantabhāvaṃ gatena. Appamāṇenāti vaḍḍhitappamāṇena. Averenāti [P 4.0172] akusalaverapuggalaverarahitena. Abyāpajjhenāti niddukkhena vigatadomanassena. So idhāti so anupaṭṭhitakāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhāno bhikkhu evaṃ kareyya, mādiso kathaṃ evarūpaṃ karissati, bhanteti paṭhamaṃ sīhanādaṃ nadi. Evaṃ sabbattha yojanā veditabbā.

'Gūthagataṃ' means feces itself. In the remaining terms as well, this is the same method. 'Pathavīsamena' means equal to the earth in the sense of not being angry. For the earth does not create joy, thinking, 'They place clean things on me,' nor does it create displeasure, thinking, 'They place unclean things on me.' This shows the meaning: 'My mind is also of such a nature.' 'Vipulena' means not small. 'Mahaggatena' means having gone to a state of greatness. 'Appamāṇena' means of increased measure. 'Averena' means free from the enmity of unwholesome states and the enmity of persons. 'Abyāpajjhena' means without suffering, with displeasure departed. 'So idha' means: that monk who has not established the foundation of mindfulness which is the contemplation of the body might do thus. 'How, venerable sir, could one like me do such a thing?' This is the first lion's roar. The application should be understood in this way throughout.

Rajoharaṇanti rajasammajjanacoḷakaṃ, pādapuñchanti, tasseva nāmaṃ. Kaḷopihatthoti pacchihattho ukkhalihattho vā. Nantakavāsīti antacchinnapilotikavasano. Sūratoti sucisīlo soraccena samannāgato. Sudantoti suṭṭhu damathaṃ upagato. Suvinītoti suṭṭhu sikkhito. Na kañci hiṃsatīti visāṇādīsu gaṇhantampi piṭṭhiṃ parimajjantampi na kañci viheṭheti. Usabhachinnavisāṇasamenāti usabhassa chinnavisāṇassa cittasadisena.

'Rajoharaṇaṃ' means a cloth for wiping away dust; 'pādapuñchanaṃ' is a name for that very thing. 'Kaḷopihattho' means with a basket in hand or with a pot in hand. 'Nantakavāsī' means wearing a worn-out cloth with cut edges. 'Sūrato' means having pure virtue, endowed with gentleness. 'Sudanto' means having well attained the state of being tamed. 'Suvinīto' means well-trained. 'Na kañci hiṃsati' means he does not harass anyone, even one who is grasping his horns and so on, or one who is stroking his back. 'Usabhachinnavisāṇasamena' means like the mind of a bull with broken horns.

Aṭṭīyeyyāti aṭṭo [V 3.0262] pīḷito bhaveyya. Harāyeyyāti lajjeyya. Jiguccheyyāti jigucchaṃ āpajjeyya.

'Aṭṭīyeyya' means one would be afflicted and oppressed. 'Harāyeyya' means one would be ashamed. 'Jiguccheyya' means one would come to feel disgust.

Medakathālikanti medakathālikā vuccati sūnakārakehi yūsanikkhamanatthāya tattha tattha katachiddā thālikā. Parihareyyāti maṃsassa pūretvā [M 3.0264] ukkhipitvā gaccheyya. Chiddāvachiddanti [P 4.0173] parittamahantehi chiddehi samannāgataṃ. Uggharantanti uparimukhehi chiddehi nikkhamamānayūsaṃ. Paggharantanti adhomukhehi nikkhamamānayūsaṃ. Evamassa sakalasarīraṃ yūsamakkhitaṃ bhaveyya. Chiddāvachiddanti navahi vaṇamukhehi parittamahanta chiddaṃ. Evamettha aṭṭhamanavamehi dvīhi aṅgehi thero attano sarīre nicchandarāgataṃ kathesi.

'Medakathālikā': a pot with holes made in it here and there by butchers for the purpose of draining out broth is called a 'medakathālikā.' 'Parihareyya' means having filled it with meat, having lifted it, one would go. 'Chiddāvachiddaṃ' means endowed with small and large holes. 'Uggharantaṃ' means having broth coming out from the upper-facing holes. 'Paggharantaṃ' means having broth coming out from the lower-facing holes. Thus his entire body would be smeared with broth. 'Chiddāvachiddaṃ' means having small and large holes, which are the nine wound-openings. Thus, here, with these two factors, the eighth and ninth, the Elder declared the absence of desire and lust in his own body.

Atha kho so bhikkhūti evaṃ therena navahi kāraṇehi sīhanāde nadite atha so bhikkhu. Accayoti aparādho. Maṃ accagamāti maṃ atikkamma abhibhavitvā pavatto. Patiggaṇhatūti khamatu. Āyatiṃ saṃvarāyāti anāgate saṃvaraṇatthāya, puna evarūpassa aparādhassa akaraṇatthāya. Tagghāti ekaṃsena. Yathādhammaṃ paṭikarosīti yathā dhammo ṭhito, tatheva karosi, khamāpesīti vuttaṃ hoti. Taṃ te mayaṃ paṭiggaṇhāmāti taṃ tava aparādhaṃ mayaṃ khamāma. Vuddhihesā bhikkhu ariyassa vinayeti esā bhikkhu ariyassa vinaye buddhassa bhagavato sāsane vuḍḍhi nāma. Katamā? Accayaṃ accayato disvā yathādhammaṃ paṭikaritvā āyatiṃ saṃvarāpajjanā. Desanaṃ pana puggalādhiṭṭhānaṃ karonto yo accayaṃ accayato [P 4.0174] disvā yathādhammaṃ paṭikaroti, āyatiṃ saṃvaraṃ āpajjatīti āha. Phalatīti sace hi thero na khameyya, tassa bhikkhuno tattheva sattadhā muddhā phaleyya. Tasmā bhagavā evamāha. Sace maṃ soti sace maṃ ayaṃ bhikkhu khamāhīti evaṃ vadati. Khamatu ca me soti ayampi cāyasmā mayhaṃ khamatūti evaṃ thero tassa accayaṃ paṭiggaṇhitvā sayampi taṃ satthu sammukhe khamāpesīti.

Then that monk: when the Elder had thus proclaimed the lion's roar with nine reasons, then that monk... 'Accayo' means an offense. 'Maṃ accagamā' means having transgressed and overwhelmed me, it occurred. 'Paṭiggaṇhatu' means may he forgive. 'Āyatiṃ saṃvarāya' means for the sake of restraint in the future, for the purpose of not committing such an offense again. 'Taggha' means certainly. 'Yathādhammaṃ paṭikarosi' means you act just as the Dhamma stands; you cause him to forgive, this is what is said. 'Taṃ te mayaṃ paṭiggaṇhāma' means we forgive that offense of yours. 'Vuddhihesā bhikkhu ariyassa vinaye' means this, monk, is called growth in the Discipline of the Noble One, in the teaching of the Blessed One, the Buddha. What is that growth? Seeing an offense as an offense, making amends according to the Dhamma, and undertaking restraint in the future. But, making the teaching based on the individual, he said: 'Whoever, seeing an offense as an offense, makes amends according to the Dhamma, and undertakes restraint in the future...' As for 'phalati': if the Elder were not to forgive, that monk's head would split into seven pieces right there on the spot. Therefore, the Blessed One said thus. 'Sace maṃ so' means if this monk says to me, 'Forgive me.' 'Khamatu ca me so' means 'May this venerable one also forgive me.' Thus the Elder, having accepted his offense, himself had that monk forgive him in the presence of the Teacher.

2. Saupādisesasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Explanation of the Saupādisesa Sutta

12. Dutiye saupādisesanti saupādānasesaṃ. Anupādisesanti upādānasesarahitaṃ niggahaṇaṃ. Mattaso kārīti pamāṇakārī na paripūrakārī. Na tāvāyaṃ, sāriputta, dhammapariyāyo paṭibhāsīti appaṭibhānaṃ nāma bhagavato natthi, na tāvāhaṃ imaṃ dhammapariyāyaṃ kathesinti ayaṃ panettha [V 3.0263] attho. Māyimaṃ dhammapariyāyaṃ sutvā pamādaṃ āhariṃsūti “mayaṃ kira catūhi apāyehi muttā”ti upari arahattatthāya vīriyaṃ akarontā mā pamādaṃ āpajjiṃsu. Pañhādhippāyena bhāsitoti tayā pucchitapañhassa sabhāvena [M 3.0265] kathitoti dasseti. Imesaṃ pana navannaṃ puggalānaṃ bhavesu chandarāgavinodanatthaṃ etameva atthuppattiṃ katvā – “seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, appamattakopi gūtho duggandho hoti, evameva kho khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, appamattakampi bhavaṃ na [P 4.0175] vaṇṇemi antamaso accharāsaṅghātamattampī”ti imaṃ suttaṃ (a. ni. 1.321) abhāsi. Na kevalañca etesaṃyeva navannaṃ puggalānaṃ gati nibaddhā, yesaṃ pana kulānaṃ tīṇi saraṇāni pañca sīlāni ekaṃ salākabhattaṃ ekaṃ pakkhiyabhattaṃ ekaṃ vassāvāsikaṃ ekā pokkharaṇī eko āvāso, evarūpāni nibaddhapuññāni atthi. Tesampi gati nibaddhā, sotāpannasadisāneva tāni kulāni.

12. In the second sutta, 'with residue remaining' means with a remainder of clinging. 'Without residue remaining' means devoid of a remainder of clinging, a departure. 'One who acts in moderation' means one who acts according to measure, not one who acts to completion. Regarding 'This exposition of the Dhamma does not yet appear to me, Sāriputta': there is no such thing as a lack of ready wit for the Blessed One; rather, the meaning here is, 'I have not yet taught this exposition of the Dhamma.' Lest, having heard this exposition of the Dhamma, they become heedless, thinking, 'We are freed from the four states of deprivation,' and not making an effort for the sake of Arahantship above, may they not fall into heedlessness. 'It was spoken with the intention of the question' shows that it was taught according to the nature of the question asked by you. Furthermore, for the sake of dispelling desire and lust in the existences of these nine individuals, having made this very arising of the matter the reason—'Just as, monks, even a small amount of excrement is foul-smelling, so too, monks, I do not praise even a small amount of existence, not even as much as a finger-snap'—he taught this sutta (Aṅguttara Nikāya 1.321). And not only is the destiny of these nine types of individuals fixed, but also of those families for whom there are established meritorious deeds such as the three refuges, the five precepts, one offering of almsfood distributed by ticket, one fortnightly offering of almsfood, one rains-residence robe, one pond, and one monastery. Their destiny, too, is fixed; those families are just like stream-enterers.

3. Koṭṭhikasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Explanation of the Koṭṭhika Sutta

13. Tatiye diṭṭhadhammavedanīyanti imasmiṃ yevattabhāve vipaccanakakammaṃ. Samparāyavedanīyanti dutiye attabhāve vipaccanakakammaṃ. Sukhavedanīyanti sukhavedanājanakakammaṃ. Dukkhavedanīyanti dukkhavedanājanakakammaṃ. Paripakkavedanīyanti laddhavipākavāraṃ. Aparipakkavedanīyanti aladdhavipākavāraṃ. Bahuvedanīyanti bahuvipākadāyakaṃ. Appavedanīyanti na bahuvipākadāyakaṃ. Avedanīyanti vipākavedanāya adāyakaṃ. Imasmiṃ sutte vaṭṭavivaṭṭaṃ kathitaṃ.

13. In the third sutta, 'experienced in the present life' refers to kamma that ripens in this very existence. 'Experienced in the next life' refers to kamma that ripens in the second existence. 'Experienced as pleasant' refers to kamma that produces pleasant feeling. 'Experienced as painful' refers to kamma that produces painful feeling. 'Ripe for experiencing' refers to having obtained its turn for giving result. 'Not ripe for experiencing' refers to not having obtained its turn for giving result. 'Producing much result' refers to giving much result. 'Producing little result' refers to not giving much result. 'Producing no result' refers to not giving resultant feeling. In this sutta, the round of existence and the escape from the round are taught.

4. Samiddhisuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Samiddhi Sutta

14. Catutthe samiddhīti attabhāvasamiddhatāya evaṃladdhanāmo therassa saddhivihārikatthero. Kimārammaṇāti kiṃpaccayā. Saṅkappavitakkāti saṅkappabhūtā vitakkā. Nāmarūpārammaṇāti nāmarūpapaccayā.
