Pathaviyaṃ pathavisaññā vibhūtā hotīti pathavārammaṇe uppannā catukkapañcakajjhānasaññā vibhūtā pākaṭā hoti. “Vibhūtā, bhante, rūpasaññā avibhūtā aṭṭhikasaññā”ti imasmiñhi sutte samatikkamassa atthitāya vibhūtatā vuttā, idha pana vipassanāvasena aniccadukkhānattato diṭṭhattā vibhūtā nāma jātā. Āposaññādīsupi eseva nayo. Evamettha heṭṭhā viya samāpattivasena samatikkamaṃ avatvā vipassanācāravasena samatikkamo vutto. Evaṃ jhāyīti evaṃ vipassanāpaṭipāṭiyā āgantvā uppāditāya phalasamāpattiyā jhāyanto.

And how is Saddha's contemplation like that of a thoroughbred? This means: how is the contemplation of a Sindhu horse, which knows cause and non-cause? It is as when, having considered it as similar to debt, bondage, loss of wealth, and a great offense called 'ruin,' one sees the falling of the goad before oneself, which is known as its 'swallowing'—this is the meaning. 'He does not contemplate relying on earth' means that due to the absence of craving for the bliss of attainment, he does not contemplate with the perception of the fourfold and fivefold jhāna having earth as its object; precisely because of the absence of craving, he is called a thoroughbred. 'And yet he contemplates' means he contemplates with the fruition attainment that has Nibbāna as its object. 'In earth, the earth-perception becomes distinct' means the perception of the fourfold and fivefold jhāna that arose with earth as its object becomes distinct, apparent. Indeed, in this sutta, it is said, 'Distinct, venerable sir, is the perception of form; indistinct is the perception of bone,' where 'distinctness' is spoken of due to the existence of transcendence. Here, however, it has become 'distinct' because it is seen as impermanent, suffering, and not-self by way of insight. In the case of the perception of water and so on, this is the same method. Thus, here, unlike in the preceding suttas, transcendence is not spoken of by way of attainment, but transcendence is spoken of by way of the practice of insight. 'Thus contemplating' means contemplating with the fruition attainment produced by proceeding through the sequence of insight.

10. Moranivāpasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Moranivāpa Sutta

10. Dasame [P 5.0081] accantaniṭṭhoti antaṃ atītattā accantasaṅkhātaṃ avināsadhammaṃ nibbānaṃ niṭṭhā assāti accantaniṭṭho. Iminā nayena sesapadāni veditabbāni. Janetasminti janitasmiṃ[V 3.0343], pajāyāti attho. Ye gottapaṭisārinoti ye janā tasmiṃ gotte paṭisaranti “ahaṃ gotamo, ahaṃ kassapo”ti, tesu loke gottapaṭisārīsu khattiyo seṭṭho. Anumatā mayāti mama sabbaññutaññāṇena saddhiṃ saṃsandetvā desitā mayā anuññātā. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānatthamevāti.

10. In the tenth sutta, as for 'accantaniṭṭho': because he has gone beyond the limit, for him there is the conclusion which is Nibbāna, the indestructible state, called 'the ultimate'; therefore, he is 'accantaniṭṭho'. By this method, the remaining terms should be understood. As for 'janetasmiṃ', it means in that which is born, that is, among the multitude of beings. As for 'ye gottapaṭisārino': those people who resort to their lineage, thinking, 'I am a Gotama,' 'I am a Kassapa'—among those in the world who resort to lineage, the khattiya is the best. As for 'anumatā mayā': having compared it with my omniscient knowledge, it was taught and permitted by me. The rest has a clear meaning everywhere.

Nissayavaggo paṭhamo.

The First Chapter on Dependence.

2. Anussativaggo

2. The Chapter on Recollections

1-2. Mahānāmasuttadvayavaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the Two Mahānāma Suttas

11-12. Dutiyassa paṭhame nānāvihārehi viharatanti gihīnaṃ nibaddho eko vihāro nāma natthi, tasmā amhākaṃ anibaddhavihārena viharantānaṃ kena vihārena katarena nibaddhavihārena vihātabbanti pucchati. Ārādhakoti sampādako paripūrako. Dhammasotasamāpanno buddhānussatiṃ [M 3.0345] bhāvetīti dhammasotasamāpanno hutvā buddhānussatiṃ bhāveti. Dutiye gilānā vuṭṭhitoti gilāno hutvā vuṭṭhito.

11-12. In the first sutta of the second vagga, as for 'dwelling with various dwellings': for householders, there is no such thing as a single, fixed dwelling. Therefore, he asks: 'For us, who dwell without a fixed dwelling, by what dwelling, by which fixed dwelling, should we dwell?' As for 'ārādhako', it means one who accomplishes, one who fulfills. As for 'having attained the stream of the Dhamma, he cultivates the recollection of the Buddha', it means: having attained the stream of the Dhamma, he cultivates the recollection of the Buddha. In the second sutta, as for 'recovered from illness', it means: having been ill, he has recovered.

3. Nandiyasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Nandiya Sutta

13. Tatiye kalyāṇamitteti sumitte. Evamettha kalyāṇamittavasena saṅghānussati kathitā. Kabaḷīkārāhārabhakkhānanti kāmāvacaradevānaṃ. Asamayavimuttoti [P 5.0082] asamayavimuttiyā vimutto khīṇāsavo.

13. In the third sutta, as for 'kalyāṇamitte', it means good friends. Thus, here, the recollection of the Saṅgha is taught by way of good friends. As for 'the eating of solid food', this refers to the sense-sphere devas. As for 'asamayavimutto', it means an Arahant liberated by timeless liberation.

4. Subhūtisuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Subhūti Sutta

14. Catutthe [V 3.0344] ko nāmāyaṃ subhūtī bhikkhūti jānantopi satthā kathāsamuṭṭhāpanatthaṃ pucchati. Sudattassa upāsakassa puttoti anāthapiṇḍikaṃ sandhāyāha. Anāthapiṇḍikassa hi putto attano cūḷapitu santike pabbajito, atha naṃ subhūtitthero ādāya satthu santikaṃ agamāsi. Saddhāpadānesūti saddhānaṃ puggalānaṃ apadānesu lakkhaṇesu.

14. In the fourth sutta, as for 'Who is this monk named Subhūti?': although the Teacher knew, he asked in order to give rise to a talk. As for 'the son of the lay follower Sudatta', this refers to Anāthapiṇḍika. For Anāthapiṇḍika's son went forth in the presence of his paternal uncle; then the Elder Subhūti, having taken him, went to the Teacher's presence. As for 'saddhāpadānesu', it means in the characteristics which are the grounds of faithful persons.

5. Mettasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Mettā Sutta

15. Pañcame sukhaṃ supatīti yathā sesajanā samparivattamānā kākacchamānā dukkhaṃ supanti, evaṃ asupitvā sukhaṃ supati. Niddaṃ okkamantopi samāpattiṃ samāpanno viya hoti. Sukhaṃ paṭibujjhatīti yathā aññe nitthunantā vijambhamānā samparivattantā dukkhaṃ paṭibujjhanti, evaṃ appaṭibujjhitvā vikasamānaṃ viya padumaṃ sukhaṃ nibbikāro paṭibujjhati. Na pāpakaṃ supinaṃ passatīti supinaṃ passantopi bhaddakameva supinaṃ passati, cetiyaṃ vandanto viya pūjaṃ karonto viya ca dhammaṃ suṇanto viya ca hoti. Yathā panaññe attānaṃ corehi samparivāritaṃ viya vāḷehi upaddutaṃ viya papāte patantaṃ viya ca passanti, na evaṃ pāpakaṃ supinaṃ passati.

15. In the fifth sutta, as for 'he sleeps happily': just as other people, tossing and turning and making noises like crows, sleep with difficulty, he does not sleep like that, but sleeps happily. Even when falling asleep, he is as if having entered a meditative attainment. As for 'he wakes happily': just as others, groaning, yawning, and tossing and turning, wake with difficulty, he does not wake like that, but wakes happily and undisturbed, like a blooming lotus. As for 'he does not see a bad dream': even when seeing a dream, he sees only a good dream; it is as if he is venerating a cetiya, making an offering, or listening to the Dhamma. Whereas others see themselves as if surrounded by thieves, oppressed by wild beasts, or falling from a cliff, he does not see a bad dream like that.

Manussānaṃ piyo hotīti ure āmukkamuttāhāro viya sīse piḷandhitamālā viya ca manussānaṃ piyo hoti manāpo. Amanussānaṃ [P 5.0083] piyo hotīti yatheva manussānaṃ, amanussānampi piyo hoti visākhatthero viya. Vatthu visuddhimagge (visuddhi. 1.258) mettākammaṭṭhānaniddese vitthāritameva. Devatā rakkhantīti puttamiva mātāpitaro devatā rakkhanti. Nāssa aggi [M 3.0346] vā visaṃ vā satthaṃ vā kamatīti mettāvihārissa kāye uttarāya upāsikāya viya aggi vā, saṃyuttabhāṇakacūḷasīvattherasseva visaṃ vā, saṃkiccasāmaṇerasseva satthaṃ vā na kamati nappavisati, nāssa kāyaṃ vikopetīti vuttaṃ hoti. Dhenuvatthumpi cettha kathayanti. Ekā kira dhenu vacchakassa khīradhāraṃ muñcamānā aṭṭhāsi. Eko luddako “taṃ vijjhissāmī”ti hatthena samparivattetvā dīghadaṇḍaṃ sattiṃ muñci. Sā tassā sarīraṃ āhacca tālapaṇṇaṃ viya vaṭṭamānā gatā[V 3.0345], neva upacārabalena na appanābalena, kevalaṃ vacchake balavahitacittatāya. Evaṃ mahānubhāvā mettā.

As for 'he is dear to humans': like a string of pearls worn on the chest or a garland worn on the head, he is dear and pleasing to humans. As for 'he is dear to non-humans': just as he is dear to humans, so too is he dear to non-humans, like the Elder Visākha. The story is elaborated in the Visuddhimagga in the exposition of the loving-kindness meditation subject. As for 'deities protect him': deities protect him just as parents protect a child. As for 'fire, poison, or a weapon does not affect him': for one who dwells with loving-kindness, fire does not affect his body, as with the laywoman Uttarā; or poison, as with the Elder Cūḷasīva, the reciter of the Saṃyutta Nikāya; or a weapon, as with the novice Saṅkicca. It does not enter, it does not destroy his body—this is the intended meaning. Here they also tell the story of a cow. A certain cow, it is said, stood releasing a stream of milk for her calf. A hunter, thinking, 'I will shoot her,' having brandished it with his hand, released a long-shafted spear. Having struck her body, it went away, turning like a palm leaf. It was not due to the power of access concentration nor the power of absorption that it did not enter; it was simply because of her mind of powerful goodwill towards her calf. Thus, loving-kindness has great power.

Tuvaṭaṃ cittaṃ samādhiyatīti mettāvihārino khippameva cittaṃ samādhiyati, natthi tassa dandhāyitattaṃ. Mukhavaṇṇo vippasīdatīti bandhanā pavuttatālapakkaṃ viya cassa vippasannavaṇṇaṃ mukhaṃ hoti. Asammūḷho kālaṃ karotīti mettāvihārino sammohamaraṇaṃ nāma natthi, asammūḷho pana niddaṃ okkamanto viya kālaṃ karoti. Uttari appaṭivijjhantoti mettāsamāpattito uttari arahattaṃ [P 5.0084] adhigantuṃ asakkonto ito cavitvā suttappabuddho viya brahmalokaṃ upapajjatīti.

As for 'the mind quickly becomes concentrated': the mind of one who dwells with loving-kindness quickly becomes concentrated; there is no slowness for him. As for 'the facial complexion brightens': his face has a very clear complexion, like a ripe palmyra fruit just fallen from its stalk. As for 'he dies unconfused': for one who dwells with loving-kindness, there is no such thing as a death with delusion; rather, being unconfused, he passes away as if falling asleep. As for 'not penetrating further': being unable to attain Arahantship beyond the attainments of loving-kindness, having passed away from here, he is reborn in the Brahma world like one awakened from sleep.

6. Aṭṭhakanāgarasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Aṭṭhakanāgara Sutta

16. Chaṭṭhe dasamoti jātigottavasena ceva sārapattakulagaṇanāya ca dasame ṭhāne gaṇīyati, tenassa dasamotveva nāmaṃ jātaṃ. Aṭṭhakanāgaroti aṭṭhakanagaravāsī. Kukkuṭārāmeti kukkuṭaseṭṭhinā kārite ārāme.

16. In the sixth sutta, as for 'Dasama': he is counted in the tenth place by reason of his lineage and clan, and by the count of families that have attained excellence. Therefore, the name 'Dasama' arose for him. 'Aṭṭhakanāgaro' means a resident of the city of Aṭṭhaka. 'In Kukkuṭārāma' means in the monastery caused to be built by the merchant Kukkuṭa.

Tena bhagavatā…pe… sammadakkhātoti ettha ayaṃ saṅkhepattho – yo so bhagavā samatiṃsa pāramiyo pūretvā sabbakilese bhañjitvā anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho, tena bhagavatā tesaṃ tesaṃ sattānaṃ āsayānusayaṃ jānatā, hatthatale ṭhapitaṃ āmalakaṃ viya sabbañeyyadhamme passatā, apica pubbenivāsādīhi jānatā, dibbena cakkhunā passatā, tīhi vā vijjāhi chahi vā pana abhiññāhi jānatā, sabbattha appaṭihatena samantacakkhunā passatā, sabbadhammajānanasamatthāya paññāya jānatā, sabbasattānaṃ cakkhuvisayātītāni tirokuṭṭādigatāni cāpi [M 3.0347] rūpāni ativisuddhena maṃsacakkhunā vā passatā, attahitasādhikāya samādhipadaṭṭhānāya paṭivedhapaññāya jānatā, parahitasādhikāya karuṇāpadaṭṭhānāya desanāpaññāya passatā, antarāyikadhamme vā jānatā, niyyānikadhamme passatā, arīnaṃ hatattā arahatā, sammā sāmaṃ sabbadhammānaṃ buddhattā sammāsambuddhenāti evaṃ catuvesārajjavasena catūhi kāraṇehi thomitena atthi nu kho eko dhammo akkhātoti.

Herein, this is the summary meaning of 'by that Blessed One... rightly proclaimed': That Blessed One, who, having fulfilled the thirty perfections, having shattered all defilements, fully awakened to unsurpassed perfect enlightenment; by that Blessed One, who knows the inclinations and latent tendencies of those various beings, who sees all knowable phenomena as clearly as a myrobalan fruit placed on the palm of the hand; and further, who knows by means of the knowledge of past lives and so forth, who sees with the divine eye, or who knows by the three kinds of knowledge or the six higher knowledges, who sees with the all-seeing eye that is unobstructed everywhere, who knows with the wisdom capable of understanding all phenomena, or who sees even forms beyond the range of the physical eye, such as those behind walls, with the supremely purified physical eye; who knows with the wisdom of penetration which accomplishes one's own welfare and has concentration as its proximate cause, who sees with the wisdom of teaching which accomplishes the welfare of others and has compassion as its proximate cause; or who knows obstructive phenomena and sees liberating phenomena; who is an Arahant because of having destroyed the enemies (the defilements), and a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One because of having rightly and by himself awakened to all phenomena—thus praised by means of the four kinds of intrepidity and by four reasons—is there a single Dhamma taught by him?

Abhisaṅkhatanti [V 3.0346] kataṃ uppāditaṃ. Abhisañcetayitanti cetayitaṃ kappayitaṃ. So [P 5.0085] tattha ṭhitoti so tasmiṃ samathavipassanādhamme ṭhito. Dhammarāgena dhammanandiyāti padadvayenapi samathavipassanāsu chandarāgo vutto. Samathavipassanāsu hi sabbena sabbaṃ chandarāgaṃ pariyādiyituṃ sakkonto arahā hoti, asakkonto anāgāmī hoti. So samathavipassanāsu chandarāgassa appahīnattā catutthajjhānacetanāya suddhāvāse nibbattati. Ayaṃ ācariyānaṃ samānatthakathā.

'Fabricated' means made, produced. 'Intended' means willed, arranged. 'He remains there' means he abides in that Dhamma of serenity and insight. By the two terms 'delight in the Dhamma' and 'joy in the Dhamma,' desire and passion for serenity and insight is stated. For one who is able to completely exhaust all desire and passion for serenity and insight becomes an Arahant; one who is unable becomes a Non-returner. Because desire and passion for serenity and insight have not been abandoned, he is reborn in the Pure Abodes through the volition associated with the fourth jhāna. This is the consistent explanation of the teachers.

Vitaṇḍavādī panāha – “teneva dhammarāgenāti vacanato akusalena suddhāvāse nibbattatī”ti. So “suttaṃ āharāhī”ti vattabbo. Addhā aññaṃ apassanto idameva āharissati. Tato vattabbo “kimpanidaṃ suttaṃ nītatthaṃ, udāhu neyyattha”nti. Addhā “nītattha”nti vakkhati. Tato vattabbo – evaṃ sante anāgāmiphalatthikena samathavipassanāsu chandarāgo kattabbo bhavissati, chandarāge uppādite anāgāmiphalaṃ paṭiladdhaṃ bhavissati, mā “suttaṃ me laddha”nti yaṃ vā taṃ vā dīpehi. Pañhaṃ kathentena hi ācariyassa santike uggahetvā attharasaṃ paṭivijjhitvā kathetuṃ vaṭṭati. Akusalena hi sagge, kusalena ca apāye paṭisandhi nāma natthi. Vuttañcetaṃ bhagavatā –

A sophist, however, says: 'Because of the saying "through that very delight in the Dhamma," one is reborn in the Pure Abodes through an unwholesome state.' He should be told: 'Bring forth the sutta.' Surely, not seeing another, he will bring forth this very one. Then he should be told: 'Now, is this sutta of definitive meaning, or is it of a meaning that needs to be inferred?' Surely, he will say: 'It is of definitive meaning.' Then he should be told: 'If that is so, then desire and passion for serenity and insight should be cultivated by one who desires the fruit of non-returning. When desire and passion are aroused, the fruit of non-returning will be attained. Do not, saying "I have found a sutta," point to just anything. For one who speaks on a question should learn it in the presence of a teacher, penetrate its essential meaning, and then speak. Indeed, there is no such thing as rebirth-linking in a heavenly world through an unwholesome state, nor in a plane of misery through a wholesome state. And this was said by the Blessed One:

“Na, bhikkhave, lobhajena kammena, dosajena kammena, mohajena kammena devā paññāyanti, manussā paññāyanti, yā vā panaññāpi kāci sugatiyo. Atha kho, bhikkhave, lobhajena kammena, dosajena kammena, mohajena kammena nirayo paññāyati, tiracchānayoni paññāyati, pettivisayo paññāyati, yā vā panaññāpi kāci duggatiyo”ti (a. ni. 6.39) –

'Monks, it is not through kamma born of greed, kamma born of hatred, or kamma born of delusion that gods are made known, or human beings are made known, or any other good destinations. But rather, monks, it is through kamma born of greed, kamma born of hatred, and kamma born of delusion that hell is made known, the animal realm is made known, the sphere of ghosts is made known, or any other bad destinations.'

Evaṃ [M 3.0348] saññāpetabbo. Sace sañjānāti, sañjānātu. No ce sañjānāti, “gaccha pātova vihāraṃ pavisitvā yāguṃ pivā”ti uyyojetabbo.

He should be made to understand thus. If he understands, let him understand. If he does not understand, he should be dismissed with the words: 'Go, enter the monastery early in the morning and drink your gruel.'

Ayaṃ kho, gahapati, ekadhammo akkhātoti ekaṃ dhammaṃ [P 5.0086] pucchitena “ayampi ekadhammo akkhāto, ayampi ekadhammo akkhāto”ti evaṃ pucchāvasena kathitattā ekādasapi dhammā ekadhammo nāma kato. Amatuppattiatthena vā sabbepi ekadhammoti vattuṃ vaṭṭati.

As for the phrase, 'This, householder, is the one Dhamma that has been taught': Since he who was asked about one Dhamma spoke in the manner of a question, saying, 'This too is one Dhamma taught... this too is one Dhamma taught...,' for this reason even the eleven dhammas are designated as 'one Dhamma.' Or, because of the meaning of being a cause for the arising of the deathless, it is fitting to call all of them 'one Dhamma.'

Nidhimukhaṃ [V 3.0347] gavesantoti nidhiṃ pariyesanto. Sakidevāti ekappayogeneva. Kathaṃ pana ekappayogeneva ekādasannaṃ nidhīnaṃ adhigamo hotīti? Idhekacco araññe jīvitavuttiṃ gavesamāno carati. Tamenaṃ aññataro atthacarako disvā “kiṃ, bho, carasī”ti pucchati. So “jīvitavuttiṃ pariyesāmī”ti āha. Itaro “tena hi samma āgaccha, etaṃ pāsāṇaṃ pavaṭṭehī”ti āha. So taṃ pavaṭṭetvā uparūpariṭṭhitā vā kucchiyā kucchiṃ āhacca ṭhitā vā ekādasa kumbhiyo passati. Evaṃ ekappayogena ekādasannaṃ adhigamo hoti.

'Seeking the mouth of the treasure' means searching for treasure. 'Only once' means with a single application of effort. But how is the attainment of eleven treasures achieved with just a single application of effort? Here, a certain person wanders in the forest seeking a livelihood. A certain benefactor sees him and asks, 'Friend, why do you wander?' He replies, 'I am searching for a livelihood.' The other says, 'Well then, friend, come and roll away this stone.' Having rolled it away, he sees eleven pots, either placed one on top of another or standing touching side to side. Thus, with a single application of effort, the attainment of the eleven is achieved.

Ācariyadhanaṃ pariyesissantīti aññatitthiyā hi yassa santike sippaṃ uggaṇhanti, tassa sippuggahaṇato pure vā pacchā vā antarantarā vā gehato nīharitvā dhanaṃ denti. Yesaṃ gehe natthi, te ñātisabhāgato pariyesanti. Yesaṃ tampi natthi, te sabhāgato pariyesanti. Tathā alabhamānā bhikkhampi caritvā dentiyeva. Taṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ.

'They will seek the teacher's fee': For the followers of other teachings, when they learn a craft from a certain teacher, they bring wealth from their homes and give it to him, either before learning the craft, or after, or from time to time. Those who have no wealth at home seek it from their like-minded relatives. Those who do not have that either, seek it from their community. Failing to obtain it thus, they even go for alms and give that. This was said in reference to that.

Kiṃ panāhanti bāhirakā tāva aniyyānikepi sāsane sippamattadāyakassa dhanaṃ pariyesanti, ahaṃ pana evaṃvidhe niyyānikasāsane ekādasavidhaṃ amatuppattipaṭipadaṃ [P 5.0087] desentassa ācariyassa pūjaṃ kiṃ na karissāmi, karissāmiyevāti vadati. Paccekaṃ dussayugena acchādesīti ekamekassa bhikkhuno ekekaṃ dussayugaṃ adāsīti attho. Samudācāravacanaṃ panettha evarūpaṃ hoti, tasmā acchādesīti vuttaṃ. Pañcasataṃ vihāranti pañcasatagghanikaṃ paṇṇasālaṃ kāresīti attho.

What is 'But I...'? First, outsiders, even in a teaching that is not a means of escape, seek wealth for a teacher who imparts a craft. As for me, in such a teaching that is a means of escape, for the teacher who expounds the elevenfold practice for the attainment of the deathless, why should I not make an offering? I shall indeed make it, he says. 'He clothed each with a pair of robes' means he gave a pair of robes to each and every monk. Herein, such is the conventional expression; therefore, it is said, 'he clothed'. 'A dwelling of five hundred' means he had a leaf-hut worth five hundred constructed.

7. Gopālasuttavaṇṇanā

7. The Explanation of the Gopāla Sutta.

17. Sattame tisso kathā ekanāḷikā caturassā nisinnavattikāti. Tattha pāḷiṃ vatvā ekekassa padassa atthakathanaṃ ekanāḷikā nāma[M 3.0349]. Apaṇḍitagopālakaṃ dassetvā, apaṇḍitabhikkhuṃ dassetvā, paṇḍitagopālakaṃ dassetvā, paṇḍitabhikkhuṃ dassetvāti catukkaṃ bandhitvā kathanaṃ caturassā nāma. Apaṇḍitagopālakaṃ dassetvā pariyosānagamanaṃ, apaṇḍitabhikkhuṃ dassetvā pariyosānagamanaṃ, paṇḍitagopālakaṃ dassetvā pariyosānagamanaṃ, paṇḍitabhikkhuṃ dassetvā pariyosānagamananti ayaṃ nisinnavattikā nāma. Ayaṃ idha sabbācariyānaṃ āciṇṇā.

17. In the seventh sutta, there are three kinds of discourse: ekanāḷikā, caturassā, and nisinnavattikā. Therein, reciting the Pāḷi and explaining the meaning of each and every word is called ekanāḷikā. Showing the unwise cowherd, showing the unwise bhikkhu, showing the wise cowherd, and showing the wise bhikkhu—a discourse that connects this group of four is called caturassā. Showing the unwise cowherd and bringing it to a conclusion; showing the unwise bhikkhu and bringing it to a conclusion; showing the wise cowherd and bringing it to a conclusion; and showing the wise bhikkhu and bringing it to a conclusion—this is called nisinnavattikā. This, here, is the established practice of all the teachers.

Ekādasahi[V 3.0348], bhikkhave, aṅgehīti ekādasahi aguṇakoṭṭhāsehi. Gogaṇanti gomaṇḍalaṃ. Pariharitunti pariggahetvā vicarituṃ. Phātiṃ kātunti vaḍḍhiṃ āpādetuṃ. Idhāti imasmiṃ loke. Na rūpaññū hotīti gaṇanato vā vaṇṇato vā rūpaṃ na jānāti. Gaṇanato na jānāti nāma attano gunnaṃ sataṃ vā sahassaṃ vāti saṅkhyaṃ na jānāti, so gāvīsu haṭāsu vā palātāsu vā gogaṇaṃ gaṇetvā “ajja ettakā na [P 5.0088] dissantī”ti dve tīṇi gāmantarāni vā aṭaviṃ vā vicaranto na pariyesati. Aññesaṃ gāvīsu attano gogaṇaṃ paviṭṭhāsupi gogaṇaṃ gaṇetvā “imā ettikā gāvo na amhāka”nti yaṭṭhiyā pothetvā na nīharati. Tassa naṭṭhā gāviyo naṭṭhāva honti. Paragāviyo gahetvā carati. Gosāmikā disvā “ayaṃ ettakaṃ kālaṃ amhākaṃ dhenū duhī”ti tajjetvā attano gāviyo gahetvā gacchanti. Tassa gogaṇopi parihāyati, pañca gorasaparibhogatopi paribāhiro hoti. Vaṇṇato na jānāti nāma “ettikā gāvī setā, ettikā rattā, ettikā kāḷā, ettikā odātā, ettikā kabarā, ettikā nīlā”ti na jānāti. So gāvīsu haṭāsu vā palātāsu vā…pe… pañcagorasaparibhogatopi paribāhiro hoti.

'With eleven factors, O bhikkhus' means with eleven portions of deficiencies. 'A herd of cattle' means a circle of cattle. 'To manage' means to look after them and move about. 'To bring about prosperity' means to cause growth. 'Here' means in this world. 'He is not a knower of form' means he does not know the form, either by count or by color. 'Not knowing by count' means he does not know the number of his own cattle, whether it is a hundred or a thousand. When cows are lost or have fled, he does not count the herd and, thinking, 'Today, this many are not seen,' wander through two or three villages or the forest searching for them. Even when others' cows have entered his own herd, he does not count the herd and, thinking, 'These many cows are not ours,' strike them with a stick and drive them out. His lost cows remain lost. He takes others' cows and moves about with them. When the cow owners see this, they threaten him, saying, 'For this long, this man has been milking our cows,' and taking their own cows, they depart. His herd also diminishes, and he is deprived of the enjoyment of the five products of the cow. 'Not knowing by color' means he does not know, 'This many cows are white, this many are red, this many are black, this many are pure white, this many are variegated, this many are dark blue.' When cows are lost or have fled... he is deprived of the enjoyment of the five products of the cow.

Na lakkhaṇakusaloti gāvīnaṃ sarīre kataṃ dhanusattisūlādibhedaṃ lakkhaṇaṃ na jānāti. So gāvīsu haṭāsu vā palātāsu vā “ajja asukalakkhaṇā asukalakkhaṇā ca gāvo na dissantī”ti…pe… pañcagorasaparibhogatopi paribāhiro hoti.

'He is not skilled in marks' means he does not know the marks made on the bodies of the cows, which are of different kinds, such as those made by a bow, a spear, or a stake. When the cows are lost or have fled, he does not think, 'Today, cows with such-and-such a mark, and such-and-such a mark, and such-and-such a mark are not seen.' ... He is also deprived of the enjoyment of the five products of the cow.

Na āsāṭikaṃ hāretāti gunnaṃ khāṇukaṇṭakādīhi pahaṭaṭṭhānesu vaṇo hoti. Tattha nīlamakkhikā aṇḍakāni ṭhapenti, tesaṃ āsāṭikāti nāmaṃ. Tāni daṇḍakena apanetvā bhesajjaṃ dātabbaṃ hoti, bālo gopālako tathā na karoti. Tena vuttaṃ – “na āsāṭikaṃ hāretā [M 3.0350] hotī”ti. Tassa gunnaṃ vaṇā vaḍḍhanti, gambhīrā honti, pāṇakā kucchiṃ pavisanti, gāvo gelaññābhibhūtā neva yāvadatthaṃ tiṇaṃ khādituṃ [P 5.0089] na pānīyaṃ pātuṃ sakkonti. Tattha gunnaṃ khīraṃ chijjati, goṇānaṃ javo hāyati, ubhayesampi jīvitantarāyo hoti. Evamassa gogaṇopi parihāyati…pe… pañcagorasatopi paribāhiro hoti.

'He does not remove the fly-eggs' means: on the cattle, in places struck by stumps, thorns, and so on, a wound occurs. There, bluebottles lay their eggs, which are called āsāṭikā. Those should be removed with a small stick and medicine should be applied, but the foolish cowherd does not do so. Therefore, it is said, 'He does not remove the fly-eggs.' For him, the cattle's wounds worsen and become deep, and worms enter the belly. The cows, overcome by sickness, are unable to eat as much grass as they need, nor are they able to drink water. In that case, the cows' milk is cut off, the bulls' speed diminishes, and for both there is danger to life. Thus his herd also diminishes... he is also deprived of the enjoyment of the five cow-products.

Na vaṇaṃ paṭicchādetā hotīti gunnaṃ vuttanayeneva sañjāto vaṇo bhesajjaṃ datvā vākena vā cīrakena vā bandhitvā paṭicchādetabbo hoti. Bālagopālako taṃ na karoti. Athassa gunnaṃ [V 3.0349] vaṇehi yūsā paggharanti, tā aññamaññaṃ nighaṃsanti. Tena aññesampi vaṇā jāyanti. Evaṃ gāvo gelaññābhibhūtā neva yāvadatthaṃ tiṇāni khādituṃ…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

'He does not cover a wound' means: a wound that has arisen on the cattle in the way already mentioned should be covered by applying medicine and binding it with bark or a strip of old cloth. The foolish cowherd does not do that. Then, pus flows from the wounds of his cattle, and they rub against one another. Because of that, wounds also arise on the other cattle. In this way, the cows, overcome by sickness, are unable to eat as much grass as they need... he is deprived.

Na dhūmaṃ kattā hotīti antovasse ḍaṃsamakasādīnaṃ ussannakāle gogaṇe vajaṃ paviṭṭhe tattha tattha dhūmo kātabbo hoti. Apaṇḍitagopālako taṃ na karoti, gogaṇo sabbarattiṃ ḍaṃsādīhi upadduto niddaṃ alabhitvā punadivase araññe tattha tattha rukkhamūlādīsu nipajjitvā niddāyati. Neva yāvadatthaṃ tiṇāni khādituṃ…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

'He is not one who makes smoke' means: during the rainy season, at a time when gadflies, mosquitoes, and so on are abundant, when the herd of cattle has entered the pen, smoke should be made in various places. The unwise cowherd does not do that. The herd of cattle, being harassed all night by gadflies and so on, does not get any sleep, and on the next day, they lie down and sleep in various places in the forest, at the roots of trees, and so on. They are not able to eat as much grass as they need... he is deprived.

Na titthaṃ jānātīti titthampi samanti vā visamanti vā sagāhanti vā niggāhanti vā na jānāti. So atitthena gāviyo otāreti. Tāsaṃ visamatitthe pāsāṇādīni akkamantīnaṃ pādā bhijjanti. Sagāhaṃ gambhīraṃ titthaṃ otiṇṇe kumbhīlādayo gāvo gaṇhanti, “ajja ettikā gāvo naṭṭhā, ajja ettikā”ti vattabbataṃ āpajjanti. Evamassa [P 5.0090] go gaṇopi parihāyati…pe… pañcagorasatopi paribāhiro hoti.

'He does not know a ford' means he does not know if a ford is even or uneven, or if it is dangerous with creatures that seize or free from such dangers. He makes the cows descend at a place that is not a proper ford. For those treading on rocks and so on at an uneven ford, their feet are broken. When they have descended into a deep, dangerous ford, crocodiles and other creatures seize the cows. It comes to be said, 'Today this many cows were lost; today that many were lost.' Thus his herd of cattle also diminishes... he is also deprived of the enjoyment of the five cow-products.

Na pītaṃ jānātīti pītampi apītampi na jānāti. Gopālakena hi “imāya gāviyā pītaṃ, imāya na pītaṃ, imāya pānīyatitthe okāso laddho, imāya na laddho”ti evaṃ pītāpītaṃ jānitabbaṃ hoti. Ayaṃ pana divasabhāge araññe gogaṇaṃ rakkhitvā “pānīyaṃ pāyessāmī”ti nadiṃ vā taḷākaṃ vā ogāhetvā gacchati. Tattha mahāusabhā ca anusabhā ca balavagāviyo ca dubbalāni ceva mahallakāni ca gorūpāni siṅgehi [M 3.0351] vā phāsukāhi vā paharitvā attano okāsaṃ katvā ūruppamāṇaṃ udakaṃ pavisitvā yathākāmaṃ pivanti. Avasesā okāsaṃ alabhamānā tīre ṭhatvā kalalamissakaṃ udakaṃ pivanti vā apītā eva vā honti. Atha so gopālako piṭṭhiyaṃ paharitvā puna araññaṃ paveseti. Tattha apītā gāviyo pipāsāya sussamānā yāvadatthaṃ tiṇāni khādituṃ na sakkonti. Tattha gunnaṃ khīraṃ chijjati. Goṇānaṃ javo hāyati…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know what has been drunk: he does not know what has been drunk and what has not been drunk. For a cowherd should know thus: 'This cow has drunk, this one has not; this one got an opportunity at the watering place, this one did not.' Thus he should know what has been drunk and not drunk. But this one, having guarded the herd in the forest during the day, thinking, 'I will have them drink water,' takes them and goes to a river or a pond. There, the great bulls, lesser bulls, and strong cows, striking the weak and old cattle with their horns or ribs, make an opportunity for themselves, enter the water thigh-deep, and drink as they please. The remaining ones, not getting an opportunity, stand on the bank and either drink water mixed with mud or remain without having drunk. Then that cowherd, striking them on the back, drives them back into the forest. There, the cows that have not drunk, being thirsty, are unable to eat grass to their satisfaction. In that situation, the milk of the cows is cut off. The swiftness of the bulls is cut off... he is excluded from the five products of the cow.

Na vīthiṃ jānātīti “ayaṃ maggo samo khemo, ayaṃ visamo sāsaṅko sappaṭibhayo”ti na jānāti. So samaṃ khemaṃ maggaṃ vajjetvā gogaṇaṃ itaramaggaṃ paṭipādeti. Tattha gāvo sīhabyagghādīnaṃ gandhena coraparissayena ca abhibhūtā bhantamigasappaṭibhāgā gīvaṃ ukkhipitvā tiṭṭhanti[V 3.0350], neva yāvadatthaṃ tiṇāni khādanti, na pānīyaṃ pivanti. Tattha gunnaṃ khīraṃ chijjati…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know the path: he does not know, 'This path is even and safe; this one is uneven, with things to be feared, and with peril.' Avoiding the even and safe path, he makes the herd take the other path. There, the cows, overwhelmed by the scent of lions, tigers, and so on, and by the danger from thieves, stand with their necks raised, like trembling wild animals. They neither eat grass to their satisfaction, nor drink water. In that situation, the milk of the cows is cut off... he is excluded from the five products of the cow.

Na [P 5.0091] gocarakusalo hotīti gopālakena hi gocarakusalena bhavitabbaṃ, pañcāhikacāro vā sattāhikacāro vā jānitabbo. Ekadisāya gogaṇaṃ cāretvā punadivase tattha na cāretabbo. Mahatā hi gogaṇena ciṇṇaṭṭhānaṃ bheritalaṃ viya suddhaṃ hoti nittiṇaṃ, udakampi ālulīyati. Tasmā pañcame vā sattame vā divase puna tattha cāretuṃ vaṭṭati. Ettakena hi tiṇampi paṭiviruhati, udakampi pasīdati, ayaṃ pana imaṃ pañcāhikacāraṃ vā sattāhikacāraṃ vā na jānāti, divase divase rakkhitaṭṭhāneyeva rakkhati. Athassa gogaṇo haritatiṇaṃ na labhati, sukkhatiṇaṃ khādanto kalalamissakaṃ udakaṃ pivati. Tattha gunnaṃ khīraṃ chijjati…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is not skilled in regard to pasture: for a cowherd should be skilled in regard to pasture; the five-day rotation or the seven-day rotation should be known. Having let the herd graze in one area, he should not let them graze there on the following day. For a place grazed by a large herd becomes bare and without grass, like the surface of a drum, and the water is also stirred up. Therefore, on the fifth or seventh day, it is suitable to let them graze there again. For by that time, the grass has grown back and the water has become clear. But this one does not know this five-day or seven-day rotation; day after day he pastures them in the very same place. Then his herd does not get green grass; eating dry grass, it drinks water mixed with mud. In that situation, the milk of the cows is cut off... he is excluded from the five products of the cow.

Anavasesadohī ca hotīti paṇḍitagopālakena hi yāva vacchakassa maṃsalohitaṃ saṇṭhāti, tāva ekaṃ dve thane ṭhapetvā sāvasesadohinā bhavitabbaṃ. Ayaṃ vacchakassa kiñci anavasesetvā duhati. Khīrapako vaccho khīrapipāsāya sussati, saṇṭhātuṃ asakkonto kampamāno mātu purato patitvā kālaṃ karonti. Mātā puttakaṃ disvā, “mayhaṃ puttako attano mātukhīraṃ pātuṃ na labhatī”ti [M 3.0352] puttasokena neva yāvadatthaṃ tiṇāni khādituṃ na pānīyaṃ pātuṃ sakkoti, thanesu khīraṃ chijjati. Evamassa gogaṇopi parihāyati…pe… pañcagorasatopi paribāhiro hoti.

And he is one who milks leaving no remainder. For a wise cowherd, for as long as the calf's flesh and blood is established, should be one who milks leaving a remainder, having set aside one or two teats. This one milks leaving nothing for the calf. The suckling calf, from thirst for milk, wastes away; unable to stand, trembling, it falls before its mother and dies. The mother, seeing her calf, due to sorrow for her offspring, thinking, 'My child does not get to drink its mother's milk,' is not able to eat grass to her satisfaction nor to drink water. The milk in her teats is cut off. Thus his herd declines... he is excluded from the five products of the cow.

Gunnaṃ pitiṭṭhānaṃ karontīti gopitaro. Gāvo pariṇāyanti yathāruciṃ gahetvā gacchantīti gopariṇāyakā. Te na [P 5.0092] atirekapūjāyāti paṇḍito hi gopālako evarūpe usabhe atirekapūjāya pūjeti, paṇītaṃ gobhattaṃ deti, gandhapañcaṅgulikehi maṇḍeti, mālaṃ piḷandheti, siṅgesu suvaṇṇarajatakosake ca dhāreti, rattiṃ dīpaṃ jāletvā celavitānassa heṭṭhā sayāpeti. Ayaṃ pana tato ekasakkārampi na karoti. Usabhā atirekapūjaṃ alabhamānā gogaṇaṃ na rakkhanti, parissayaṃ na vārenti. Evamassa gogaṇopi parihāyati…pe… pañcagorasatopi paribāhiro hoti.

Because they make a foundation for the cows, they are called 'gopitaro' (cow-protectors). Because they lead the cows, taking them and going as they please, they are called 'gopariṇāyakā' (cow-leaders). 'He does not give them excessive honor' means: a wise cowherd honors such bulls with excessive honor. He gives them choice cow-fodder, adorns them with five-fingered perfume marks, places a garland on them, and puts gold and silver sheaths on their horns. At night, having lit a lamp, he has them sleep under a cloth canopy. But this one does not perform even one of these acts of veneration. The bulls, not receiving excessive honor, do not guard the herd and do not ward off danger. Thus his herd declines... he is excluded from the five products of the cow.

Idhāti [V 3.0351] imasmiṃ sāsane. Na rūpaññū hotīti “cattāri mahābhūtāni catunnañca mahābhūtānaṃ upādāyarūpa”nti evaṃ vuttaṃ rūpaṃ dvīhākārehi na jānāti gaṇanato vā samuṭṭhānato vā. Gaṇanato na jānāti nāma – “cakkhāyatanaṃ sotāyatanaṃ ghānāyatanaṃ jivhākāyarūpasaddagandharasaphoṭṭhabbāyatanaṃ, itthindriyaṃ purisindriyaṃ jīvitindriyaṃ kāyaviññatti vacīviññatti ākāsadhātu āpodhātu rūpassa lahutā, mudutā, kammaññatā, upacayo, santati, jaratā, rūpassa aniccatā, kabaḷīkāro āhāro”ti (dha. sa. 657-665) evaṃ pāḷiyā āgatā pañcavīsati rūpakoṭṭhāsāti na jānāti. Seyyathāpi so gopālako gaṇanato gunnaṃ rūpaṃ na jānāti, tathūpamo ayaṃ bhikkhu. So gaṇanato rūpaṃ ajānanto rūpaṃ pariggahetvā arūpaṃ vavatthapetvā rūpārūpaṃ pariggahetvā paccayaṃ sallakkhetvā lakkhaṇaṃ āropetvā kammaṭṭhānaṃ matthakaṃ pāpetuṃ na sakkoti. So yathā tassa gopālakassa gogaṇo na vaḍḍhati, evaṃ imasmiṃ sāsane sīlasamādhivipassanāmaggaphalanibbānehi na vaḍḍhati. Yathā ca so gopālako pañcahi gorasehi paribāhiro hoti[P 5.0093], evamevāyaṃ asekhena sīlakkhandhena asekhena samādhipaññāvimutti vimuttiñāṇadassanakkhandhenāti pañcahi dhammakkhandhehi paribāhiro hoti.

Here means in this Dispensation. He is not one who knows form means he does not know form—spoken of as 'the four great elements and the form derived from the four great elements'—in two ways: by enumeration or by origin. By enumeration, he does not know means: he does not know the twenty-five portions of form that come in the Pāḷi text thus: 'The eye-base, ear-base, nose-base, tongue-base, body-base, form-base, sound-base, odor-base, taste-base, tangible-object-base; the femininity faculty, the masculinity faculty, the life faculty; bodily intimation, verbal intimation; the space element; lightness of form, softness of form, wieldiness of form; accumulation of form, continuity of form, decay of form, impermanence of form; and physical nutriment.' Just as that cowherd does not know the form of the cows by enumeration, this bhikkhu is of such a likeness. Not knowing form by enumeration, he is not able to comprehend form, define the formless, comprehend form-and-the-formless, discern their conditions, apply the characteristics, and bring the meditation subject to its culmination. Just as the herd of that cowherd does not grow, so in this Dispensation, he does not grow by way of virtue, concentration, insight, path, fruition, and Nibbāna. And just as that cowherd is excluded from the five products of the cow, so too is this one excluded from the five aggregates of Dhamma of one beyond training: the aggregate of virtue, the aggregate of concentration, the aggregate of wisdom, the aggregate of liberation, and the aggregate of the knowledge and vision of liberation.

Samuṭṭhānato [M 3.0353] na jānāti nāma – “ettakaṃ rūpaṃ ekasamuṭṭhānaṃ, ettakaṃ dvisamuṭṭhānaṃ, ettakaṃ tisamuṭṭhānaṃ, ettakaṃ catusamuṭṭhānaṃ, ettakaṃ nakutoci samuṭṭhātī”ti na jānāti. Seyyathāpi so gopālako vaṇṇato gunnaṃ rūpaṃ na jānāti, tathūpamo ayaṃ bhikkhu. So samuṭṭhānato rūpaṃ ajānanto rūpaṃ pariggahetvā…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know by way of origin, that is: he does not know, 'This much form has a single origin; this much has a double origin; this much has a triple origin; this much has a quadruple origin; this much does not arise from any cause.' Just as that cowherd does not know the appearance of the cows by their color, so is this bhikkhu comparable. That bhikkhu, not knowing form by way of its origin, having taken up form... is an outsider.

Na lakkhaṇakusalo hotīti “kammalakkhaṇo bālo, kammalakkhaṇo paṇḍito”ti evaṃ vuttaṃ kusalākusalakammaṃ paṇḍitabālalakkhaṇanti na jānāti. So evaṃ ajānanto bāle vajjetvā paṇḍite na sevati. Bāle vajjetvā paṇḍite asevanto kappiyākappiyaṃ kusalākusalaṃ sāvajjānavajjaṃ garukalahukaṃ satekicchātekicchaṃ kāraṇākāraṇaṃ na jānāti. Taṃ ajānanto kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti. So yathā tassa gopālakassa gogaṇo na vaḍḍhati, evaṃ imasmiṃ sāsane yathāvuttehi sīlādīhi na vaḍḍhati. So gopālako viya ca pañcahi gorasehi, pañcahi dhammakkhandhehi paribāhiro hoti.

He is not skilled in characteristics means: he does not know that wholesome and unwholesome kamma are the characteristics of the wise and the fool, as it is said: 'The fool is characterized by his kamma; the wise one is characterized by his kamma.' Not knowing this, he avoids fools but does not associate with the wise. By avoiding fools and not associating with the wise, he does not know what is allowable and unallowable, what is wholesome and unwholesome, what is blameworthy and blameless, what is a grave and a light offense, what is curable and incurable, and what is a cause and not a cause. Not knowing this, he is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it. Just as the herd of cattle of that cowherd does not grow, so too in this Dispensation, he does not grow by means of virtue and so on, as has been described. And like that cowherd, he is excluded from the five products of a cow and from the five aggregates of the Dhamma.

Na āsāṭikaṃ hāretā hotīti “uppannaṃ kāmavitakka”nti evaṃ vutte kāmavitakkādayo na [V 3.0352] vinodeti. So imaṃ akusalavitakkaṃ āsāṭikaṃ ahāretvā vitakkavasiko hutvā vicaranto kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti. So yathā tassa gopālakassa…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is not one who removes the tormenting thing means: he does not dispel sensual thoughts and so on, which are spoken of thus, 'A sensual thought has arisen.' That bhikkhu, not having dispelled this unwholesome thought, which is a tormenting thing, wanders under the sway of thought and is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it. Just as for that cowherd... he is an outsider.

Na vaṇaṃ paṭicchādetā hotīti “cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā nimittaggāhī [P 5.0094] hotī”tiādinā nayena sabbārammaṇesu nimittaṃ gaṇhanto yathā so gopālako vaṇaṃ na paṭicchādeti, evaṃ saṃvaraṃ na sampādeti. So vivaṭadvāro vicaranto kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is not one who covers a wound means: grasping the sign in all sense-objects—in the manner of 'Having seen a form with the eye, he is a grasper of the sign...'—just as that cowherd does not cover a wound, so he does not accomplish restraint. That bhikkhu, wandering with open doors, is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it... he is an outsider.

Na dhūmaṃ kattā hotīti so gopālako dhūmaṃ viya dhammadesanādhūmaṃ na karoti, dhammakathaṃ vā sarabhaññaṃ vā upanisinnakakathaṃ vā anumodanaṃ vā na karoti, tato naṃ manussā “bahussuto guṇavā”ti na jānanti. Te guṇāguṇaṃ ajānanto catūhi paccayehi saṅgahaṃ na karonti. So paccayehi kilamamāno buddhavacanaṃ sajjhāyaṃ kātuṃ vattapaṭivattaṃ pūretuṃ kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is not one who makes smoke means: like the cowherd's smoke, he does not make the smoke of the Dhamma teaching. He does not give a Dhamma talk, or a recitation with chanting, or a talk to those sitting near, or an expression of appreciation. Because of that, people do not know him as 'learned and virtuous.' Those people, not knowing his virtue or lack of virtue, do not support him with the four requisites. That bhikkhu, being afflicted by the lack of requisites, is unable to recite the Buddha's words, to fulfill his duties and obligations, or to take up a meditation subject and develop it... he is an outsider.

Na [M 3.0354] titthaṃ jānātīti titthabhūte bahussutabhikkhū na upasaṅkamati. Anupasaṅkamanto “idaṃ, bhante, byañjanaṃ kathaṃ ropetabbaṃ? Imassa bhāsitassa ko attho? Imasmiṃ ṭhāne pāḷi kiṃ vadati? Imasmiṃ ṭhāne attho kiṃ dīpetī”ti evaṃ na paripucchati na paripañhati, na jānāpetīti attho. Tassa te evaṃ aparipucchitā avivaṭañceva na vivaranti, bhājetvā na dassenti, anuttānīkatañca na uttāniṃ karonti, apākaṭaṃ na pākaṭaṃ karonti. Anekavihitesu ca kaṅkhāṭhāniyesu dhammesūti anekavidhāsu kaṅkhāsu ekakaṅkhampi na paṭivinodenti. Kaṅkhāyeva hi kaṅkhāṭhāniyā dhammā [P 5.0095] nāma. Tattha ekaṃ kaṅkhampi na nīharantīti attho. So evaṃ bahussutatitthaṃ anupasaṅkamitvā sakaṅkho kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti. Yathā vā so gopālako titthaṃ na jānāti, evaṃ ayampi bhikkhu dhammatitthaṃ na jānāti. Ajānanto avisaye pañhaṃ pucchati, ābhidhammikaṃ upasaṅkamitvā kappiyākappiyaṃ pucchati, vinayadharaṃ upasaṅkamitvā rūpārūpaparicchedaṃ pucchati. Te avisaye puṭṭhā kathetuṃ na sakkonti. So attanā sakaṅkho kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know the ford means: he does not approach the learned bhikkhus who are like a ford. Not approaching, he does not repeatedly ask, 'Venerable sir, how should this phrase be construed? What is the meaning of this saying? In this place, what does the Pāli say? In this place, what meaning does it show?' In this way, he does not repeatedly ask or inquire. For him, since he does not ask repeatedly, those learned bhikkhus do not reveal what is unrevealed, they do not explain it by analyzing it, they do not make plain what is not plain, and they do not make manifest what is not manifest. And regarding 'matters that are a basis for doubt in many ways,' this means that in the many kinds of doubts, they do not dispel even a single doubt. For doubts themselves are what are called 'matters that are a basis for doubt.' In this context, they do not remove even one doubt; this is the meaning. Thus, not having approached the ford of the learned, he, being full of doubt, is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it. Alternatively, just as that cowherd does not know the ford, so too this bhikkhu does not know the ford of the Dhamma. Not knowing, he asks questions in an inappropriate domain. He approaches an expert in Abhidhamma and asks about what is allowable and unallowable. He approaches a master of the Vinaya and asks about the analysis of form and the formless. They, being asked in an inappropriate domain, are unable to answer. That bhikkhu, being himself full of doubt, is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it... he is an outsider.

Na [V 3.0353] pītaṃ jānātīti yathā so gopālako pītāpītaṃ na jānāti, evaṃ dhammūpasañhitaṃ pāmojjaṃ na jānāti na labhati. Savanamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nissāya ānisaṃsaṃ na vindati, dhammassavanaggaṃ gantvā sakkaccaṃ na suṇāti, nisinno niddāyati, kathaṃ katheti, aññavihitako hoti. So sakkaccaṃ dhammaṃ assuṇanto kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know what has been drunk means: just as that cowherd does not know what has been drunk and what has not been drunk, so too he does not know and does not obtain the joy connected with the Dhamma. He does not find the benefit that comes from the basis for meritorious action consisting of listening. Having gone to the hall for listening to the Dhamma, he does not listen respectfully. While seated, he dozes, engages in talk, or becomes one whose mind is directed elsewhere. That bhikkhu, not listening to the Dhamma respectfully, is unable to take up a meditation subject and develop it... he is an outsider.

Na vīthiṃ jānātīti so gopālako maggāmaggaṃ viya “ayaṃ lokiyo, ayaṃ lokuttaro”ti ariyaṃ aṭṭhaṅgikaṃ maggaṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Ajānanto lokiyamagge abhinivisitvā lokuttaraṃ nibbattetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He does not know the path means: just as that cowherd does not know the path from the non-path, so he does not know the noble eightfold path as it really is, that is, 'This path is worldly; this path is supramundane.' Not knowing this, having adhered to the worldly path, he is unable to bring the supramundane path into being... he is an outsider.

Na gocarakusalo hotīti so gopālako pañcāhikasattāhikacāre viya cattāro satipaṭṭhāne “ime lokiyā, ime lokuttarā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Ajānanto sukhumaṭṭhānesu attano ñāṇaṃ carāpetvā lokiyasatipaṭṭhāne abhinivisitvā lokuttaraṃ nibbattetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is not skilled in the pasture means: just as that cowherd does not know the five-day and seven-day grazing rotations, so he does not know the four foundations of mindfulness as they really are, that is, 'These are worldly; these are supramundane.' Not knowing this, and not having made his own knowledge graze in subtle matters, he adheres to the worldly foundations of mindfulness and is unable to bring the supramundane into being... he is an outsider.

Anavasesadohī [M 3.0355][P 5.0096] hotīti paṭiggahaṇe mattaṃ ajānanto anavasesaṃ duhati. Niddesavāre panassa abhihaṭṭhuṃ pavārentīti abhiharitvā pavārenti. Ettha dve abhihārā vācābhihāro ca, paccayābhihāro ca. Vācābhihāro nāma manussā bhikkhussa santikaṃ gantvā “vadeyyātha, bhante, yenattho”ti pavārenti. Paccayābhihāro nāma vatthādīni vā sappinavanītaphāṇitādīni vā gahetvā bhikkhussa santikaṃ gantvā “gaṇhatha, bhante, yāvatakena attho”ti vadanti. Tatra bhikkhu mattaṃ na jānātīti bhikkhu tesu paccayesu pamāṇaṃ na jānāti. “Dāyakassa vaso veditabbo, deyyadhammassa vaso veditabbo, attano thāmo veditabbo”ti iminā nayena pamāṇayuttakaṃ aggahetvā yaṃ āharanti, taṃ sabbaṃ gaṇhātīti attho. Manussā vippaṭisārino na puna abhiharitvā pavārenti. So paccayehi kilamanto kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā vaḍḍhetuṃ na sakkoti…pe… paribāhiro hoti.

He is 'one who milks without leaving a remainder' because, not knowing the measure in receiving, he milks everything without remainder. In the section of exposition, 'they invite him to bring' means they bring and invite. Here, there are two kinds of bringing forward: bringing forward by speech and bringing forward of requisites. Bringing forward by speech is when people approach a bhikkhu and say, 'Please say, venerable sir, what you need,' thus inviting him. Bringing forward of requisites is when they take items such as cloth or ghee, fresh butter, molasses, etc., approach a bhikkhu, and say, 'Take, venerable sir, as much as you need.' In this case, 'the bhikkhu does not know the measure' means the bhikkhu does not know the proper amount of those requisites. The meaning is that, without taking what is appropriate in measure in the way that 'the donor's disposition should be understood, the value of the gift should be understood, and one's own capacity should be understood,' he takes everything they bring. People, feeling regret, do not bring and invite him again. Being exhausted by the requisites, he cannot take up and develop the meditation subject... he becomes an outsider.

Te na atirekapūjāya pūjetā hotīti so gopālako mahāusabhe viya there bhikkhū imāya āvi ceva raho ca mettākāyakammādikāya atirekapūjāya na pūjeti. Tato therā “ime [V 3.0354] amhesu garucittīkāraṃ na karontī”ti navake bhikkhū dvīhi saṅgahehi na saṅgaṇhanti, neva dhammasaṅgahena saṅgaṇhanti, na āmisasaṅgahena, cīvarena vā pattena vā pattapariyāpannena vā vasanaṭṭhānena vā kilamantepi nappaṭijagganti, pāḷiṃ vā aṭṭhakathaṃ vā dhammakathābandhaṃ [P 5.0097] vā guḷhaganthaṃ vā na sikkhāpenti. Navakā therānaṃ santikā sabbaso ime dve saṅgahe alabhamānā imasmiṃ sāsane patiṭṭhātuṃ na sakkonti. Yathā tassa gopālakassa gogaṇo na vaḍḍhati, evaṃ sīlādīhi na vaḍḍhanti. Yathā ca so gopālako pañcahi gorasehi, evaṃ pañcahi dhammakkhandhehi paribāhirā honti. Sukkapakkho kaṇhapakkhe vuttavipallāsavasena yojetvā veditabbo.

'He is not one who honors them with excessive honor' means: he does not honor the elder bhikkhus with excessive honor—that is, with loving bodily actions and so on, both in their presence and in their absence—in the way that a cowherd honors the great bulls. Therefore, the elders, thinking, 'These junior monks do not show us proper respect,' do not support the junior monks with the two kinds of support. They do not support them with the support of the Dhamma, nor do they support them with material support; they do not look after them with robes, bowls, the contents of the bowl, or a dwelling place, even when they are weary. They do not teach them the Pāḷi, the commentaries, the traditional line of Dhamma talks, or the profound texts. The junior monks, not receiving these two kinds of support at all from the elders, are unable to establish themselves in this Dispensation. Just as that cowherd’s herd of cows does not grow, so too they do not grow in virtue and other qualities. And just as that cowherd is excluded from the five products of the cow, so too are they excluded from the five groups of Dhamma. The white side is to be understood by applying the opposite of what was said for the black side.

Anussativaggo dutiyo.

The Second Chapter on Recollections.

Manorathapūraṇiyā aṅguttaranikāya-aṭṭhakathāya

In the Manorathapūraṇī, the Commentary to the Aṅguttara Nikāya,

Ekādasakanipātassa saṃvaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The exposition of the Eleventh Nipāta is concluded.

Nigamanakathā

Concluding Words

Ettāvatā [M 3.0356] ca –

And thus far—

Āyācito [P 5.0098] sumatinā therena bhadantajotipālena;

Kañcipurādīsu mayā pubbe saddhiṃ vasantena.

Requested by the wise elder, the venerable Jotipāla, with whom I formerly dwelt in Kañcipura and other places;

Varatambapaṇṇidīpe mahāvihāramhi vasanakālepi;

Pākaṃ gate viya dume valañjamānamhi saddhamme.

And even while dwelling in the Great Monastery on the excellent island of Tambapaṇṇi, when the good Dhamma was on the verge of ruin, like a tree struck by the wind about to fall and break;

Pāraṃ piṭakattayasāgarassa gantvā ṭhitena sumatinā;

Parisuddhājīvenābhiyācito jīvakenāpi.

and being requested again also by the wise elder named Jīvaka, whose livelihood was completely pure, and who, having crossed to the far shore of the ocean of the Tipiṭaka, stood firm;

Dhammakathāya [V 3.0355] nipuṇaparamanikāyassaṭṭhakathaṃ āraddho;

Yamahaṃ cirakālaṭṭhitimicchanto sāsanavarassa.

I, desiring the long-lasting existence of the excellent Dispensation, have undertaken to compose that commentary on the great Nikāya named Aṅguttara, which is considered a standard and has been repeatedly explored and investigated by countless Dhamma-speakers with subtle wisdom.

Sā hi mahāaṭṭhakathāya sāramādāya niṭṭhitā esā;

Catunavutiparimāṇāya pāḷiyā bhāṇavārehi.

For this commentary was completed by taking the essence from the Great Commentary, with the Pāḷi text amounting to ninety-four recitations.

Sabbāgamasaṃvaṇṇanamanoratho pūrito ca me yasmā;

Etāya manorathapūraṇīti nāmaṃ tato assā.

And since my wish to compose the explanation of all the Nikāyas has been fulfilled by this, its name has therefore become Manorathapūraṇī (The Fulfiller of Wishes).

Ekūnasaṭṭhimatto [P 5.0099] visuddhimaggopi bhāṇavārehi;

Atthappakāsanatthāya āgamānaṃ kato yasmā.

Since the Visuddhimagga, amounting to fifty-nine recitations, was also made by me for the purpose of clarifying the meaning of the Āgamas,

Tasmā tena sahāyaṃ gāthāgaṇanānayena aṭṭhakathā;

Tīhādhikadiyaḍḍhasataṃ viññeyyā bhāṇavārānaṃ.

Therefore, this commentary, together with that Visuddhimagga, should be understood, by the method of counting gāthās, as one hundred and fifty-three recitations.

Tīhādhikadiyaḍḍhasatappamāṇamiti bhāṇavārato esā;

Samayaṃ pakāsayantī mahāvihārādhivāsīnaṃ.

Thus, this commentary, amounting by recitation count to one hundred and fifty-three, reveals the doctrine of the residents of the Mahāvihāra.

Mūlaṭṭhakathāsāraṃ ādāya mayā imaṃ karontena;

Yaṃ puññamupacitaṃ tena hotu loko sadā sukhitoti.

By whatever merit has been accumulated by me in composing this work, taking the essence of the root commentary, through that may the entire world always be happy.

Paramavisuddhasaddhābuddhivīriyappaṭimaṇḍitena [M 3.0357] sīlācārajjavamaddavādiguṇasamudayasamuditena sakasamayasamayantaragahanajjhogāhanasamatthena paññāveyyattiyasamannāgatena tipiṭakapariyattippabhede sāṭṭhakathe satthu sāsane appaṭihatañāṇappabhāvena mahāveyyākaraṇena karaṇasampattijanitasukhaviniggatamadhurodāravacanalāvaṇṇayuttena yuttamuttavādinā vādīvarena mahākavinā pabhinnapaṭisambhidāparivāre chaḷabhiññādippabhedaguṇappaṭimaṇḍite uttarimanussadhamme suppatiṭṭhitabuddhīnaṃ therānaṃ theravaṃsappadīpānaṃ mahāvihāravāsīnaṃ vaṃsālaṅkārabhūtena suvipulavisuddhabuddhinā buddhaghosoti [V 3.0356][P 5.0100] garūhi gahitanāmadheyyena therena katā ayaṃ manorathapūraṇī nāma aṅguttaranikāyaṭṭhakathā –

This commentary on the Aṅguttara Nikāya, named Manorathapūraṇī, was composed by the elder who received the name Buddhaghosa from his revered teachers; who was adorned with supreme and pure faith, wisdom, and energy; endowed with a collection of virtues such as morality, good conduct, uprightness, and gentleness; capable of penetrating the thickets of his own doctrine and the doctrines of others; possessed of clarity of wisdom; having the power of unobstructed knowledge in the Teacher's Dispensation with its commentaries, which is divided into the Tipiṭaka; a great grammarian; possessing the beauty of sweet and pure speech that issues forth with ease, produced by the perfection of the vocal organs; who speaks freely and appropriately; excellent among debaters; a great poet; who was an ornament of the lineage of the elders residing in the Mahāvihāra—those elders who were lamps of the Theravāda lineage, whose wisdom was well-established in the supermundane states, adorned with qualities such as the six supernormal knowledges and surrounded by the analytical knowledges; and who possessed very extensive and pure wisdom.

Tāva tiṭṭhatu lokasmiṃ, lokanittharaṇesinaṃ;

Dassentī kulaputtānaṃ, nayaṃ cittavisuddhiyā.

May it remain in the world for so long, showing the way to the purification of mind for the noble sons who seek deliverance from the world,

Yāva buddhoti nāmampi, suddhacittassa tādino;

Lokamhi lokajeṭṭhassa, pavattati mahesinoti.

for as long as the very name 'Buddha' continues to exist in the world—the name of the great sage, the foremost in the world, the Such-like one with a pure mind.

Manorathapūraṇī nāma

The Manorathapūraṇī

Aṅguttaranikāya-aṭṭhakathā sabbākārena niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Aṅguttara Nikāya is completed in every respect.
